### feedback_cross_language_pitfalls.md

```markdown
---
name: Cross-language design pitfalls
description: Three approaches the user rejected when designing SoraMech's cross-language wire format
type: feedback
originSessionId: e9ed70c7-2b63-425c-987c-6431040bedfb
---
When designing anything that touches the cross-language data path, avoid these three shapes — the user pushed back on each and the rejections are load-bearing for the architecture.

**1. Don't propose runtime "handles" that route operations back to the originating spec.**

**Why:** Real library functions in the wild (qsort, libcurl, anything from libc) expect actual native values — a real `int (*)(int,int)` function pointer, a real `char *` — not a SoraMech-issued id string. They can't be modified to participate in our runtime's routing scheme. The wire must carry data the destination spec can fully reconstruct into a native value, not opaque tokens.

**How to apply:** When a value seems hard to serialize (function pointers, coroutines, opaque pointers), decompose to data primitives (address + signature, bytecode + upvalues, ptr + length). If the destination needs a callable, the destination spec synthesizes one at reconstruction time (libffi trampoline for C, `ffi.cast` for LuaJIT). The "phone home" callback that the trampoline performs internally is fine — it's an artifact of the destination spec's reconstruction, not exposed in the wire format. What's forbidden is a wire primitive that says "operations on this value cross back to the source."

**2. Don't propose pair-wise language conversion paths (lua→c, c→lua, etc.).**

**Why:** N languages means N² conversion paths, which means adding a new language breaks the existing ones. The wire must use a language-agnostic intermediate (JSON) and every spec must implement only the two pair-symmetric callbacks (`to_json`, `from_json`). New language = implement those two functions, nothing else touches.

**How to apply:** Never write code that names two specific languages in a conversion context. Specs only ever see JSON on the wire. If a JSON primitive needs an extension (like `$function_pointer`), it lives in the universal JSON contract, not as a lua-spec-specific or c-spec-specific concept.

**3. Don't propose per-function annotations for type ambiguities (lengths, discriminators, callback signatures).**

**Why:** The user pointed out that "discriminator" is a vocab word the system has no general way to ask about. We can't prompt "which param is the length?" because that pairing convention lives in the function's English documentation, not its types. Asking the user to write annotations means asking them to know specialized vocabulary they didn't sign up for.

**How to apply:** When a function signature has ambiguous types (`char *` with no length, `union` with no tag, `void *` with no signature), expose each parameter as a separate input slot on the box. The user wires the slots according to the function's documentation — `p` from one source, `n` from another. The system never infers pairings; the user expresses them by wiring. If the destination needs a length and no length wire is provided, the wire compile fails with a plain-English fix-it message ("destination needs length, source provides only a pointer — wire a length input here").

**Common thread:** all three rejections are the same principle in different clothes — **SoraMech bends to the language, not the other way around.** Users write idiomatic code in their language. Libraries are used as-is. Specs are self-contained and pair-symmetric. The system stays out of the user's vocabulary.

```

### project_cross_language_wire.md

```markdown
---
name: Cross-language wire format
description: Dual-ring slot per port (native ring + JSON ring + ordering ring). JSON only at language borders. Per-edge classification at graph load; per-cell precision at runtime.
type: project
originSessionId: 5670858b-274f-49c4-89b8-a4b83ce33d7c
---
Design corrected 2026-05-21 — replaces the older "JSON always" framing.

**Each input slot is a dual-ring buffer.** Per port: one `ring_native` (consumer-language native bytes), one `ring_json` (JSON bytes from cross-language producers), and one `ring_order` of `(which_ring, idx)` tuples in arrival order. Allocate `ring_json` and `ring_order` lazily — same-language-only ports pay nothing for them.

**Push selects ring by per-edge bit.** Producer's spec writes native bytes to `ring_native` if `output_edge_native[j]` is true; otherwise calls `native_to_json` and writes to `ring_json`. Either way, pushes a `(which_ring, idx)` tuple to `ring_order`.

**Pop reads ordering ring first.** Dispatch pops `ring_order`, gets `(which_ring, idx)`, then pops the indicated cell. The consumer's spec receives `(input_data[i], input_native[i])` per input port and dispatches per flag — decode native or decode JSON.

**Why dual-ring instead of per-port classification.** Per-port (slot is all-JSON if any fan-in is cross-lang) is the pessimistic approximation — same-language values eat JSON cost on mixed-fan-in ports. Dual-ring is precise per cell: a port fed by one Lua box and one C box keeps the Lua cells native and JSON-encodes only the C-sourced cells.

**Why JSON for the cross-language ring (not pair-wise paths, not type-annotated raw bytes).** JSON's structure is self-describing — every language has a natural mapping for object/array/number/string/bool/null. Adding language K means K's spec implements its own `native_to_json` and `json_to_native`. N spec-pairs, not N² language-pair paths. The native ring's format is each spec's private choice — Lua may pick msgpack or `cjson.encode` or a custom binary form; C uses `memcpy` of the wrapper struct; Bash uses strings.

**Sentinel primitives for non-JSON-mappable values.** `$ref` (pointer + length into the large-value heap), `$function_pointer`, `$lang_opaque` ("these bytes are language-X-internal"). Carried inside the JSON ring as object-shaped sentinels. Cross-language receivers that can't reconstruct fail at wire compile.

**`$function_pointer` reconstruction — wrapper binary + persistent subprocess (issue 318, amended 2026-05-22).** Earlier design had the consumer's spec FFI-cast the raw process-local address. Rejected: FFI is language-pair-specific, biases SoraMech toward the Lua↔C corner, breaks the N-not-N² rule. Replacement: at map-compile time the compiler asks the *producing* spec to compile the referenced function + its enumerable dependencies into a standalone wrapper binary on disk that speaks a small line protocol on stdio. The consumer spec spawns one persistent subprocess per `$function_pointer` it receives and talks to it over the same socket-like channel used for any out-of-process spec. Every language pays the same price; new languages only implement "compile a function to a wrapper binary" — no pair-wise FFI knowledge required.

**Spec `invoke` signature change.** Collapses `invoke_native` and `invoke_json` into one `invoke` with per-input/per-output flags: `int invoke(handle, ..., const int *input_native, int n_inputs, int output_native, void *out_buf, ...)`. The spec dispatches per input internally. This is the contract change that unblocks 306/307's typed marshalling.

**Two compile-time layers stack:**
- **Per-edge classification (issue 312, this design)** — graph loader computes `input_edge_native[]` and `output_edge_native[]` on each box; dispatch routes pushes accordingly. This issue is the wire-format design.
- **Same-language source merge (issue 313, research / blocked)** — concatenate every same-language box's source into one translation unit per language. Boxes still run as separate thread-pool tasks; what 313 thins is the spec invocation overhead inside the merged region (direct call into the worker's `lua_State` instead of dlsym + marshal + bridge). The dual-ring slot machinery does NOT collapse — tasks still need slots to wait for sibling inputs. 313 makes the ring cells cheaper to read/write; it doesn't remove them.

**Three rejected design shapes** (kept here so they don't get reinvented):
1. **Handles back-routing to the owning spec** — library functions in the wild can't be modified to use SoraMech-shaped handles. Rejected.
2. **Pair-wise language paths** — every new language would have to grow N-1 translation paths against existing languages. Rejected. The dual-ring's "two rings per slot" is the dimension-cap that prevents this.
3. **Per-function discriminator / length annotations on the wire** — the system has no universal vocabulary to ask "is param 2 the length?" Rejected; the user expresses these by wiring two separate input slots.

**How to apply:** When discussing wire format, slot store shape, dispatch routing, or spec callbacks: think dual-ring (native, JSON, ordering) per slot, per-edge classification at push time, per-cell tag at pop time, JSON-only-at-borders, language-private native bytes everywhere else.

```

### project_custom_translation_specs.md

```markdown
---
name: Custom translation specs per port
description: Users can override the default native/JSON wire path on any input port for any reason — a knob in the wire format vocabulary (issue 246)
type: project
originSessionId: 1228a221-1c09-4c26-94f3-172d7777b2b9
---
**Per-port custom translation specs** let the user intervene at a specific input port and replace the default dual-ring decode path with user-written code. The override applies to that one port; the rest of the graph uses the default native-or-JSON path from issue 312.

**Motivating cases (any reason qualifies, not just cross-language):**
- Cross-language ambiguity the default specs can't infer: a C++ class with methods landing at a C box (drop methods? expose as fn pointers? restructure?), a `void *` carrying a libcurl handle, a union whose discriminator is on a separate wire.
- Same-language reshape: massaging a value into a different shape on its way in (rename fields, drop a layer, project a subset) without adding a transform box on the canvas.
- Validation / coercion: clamp a number, enforce a schema, fail loudly on unexpected shapes.

**How it composes with the dual-ring design:**
- The custom spec runs at *pop* time on the consumer side, after the ordering ring has been consulted and the cell has been read from the appropriate ring. The user's code receives the decoded value (native or JSON-decoded) and produces whatever the consumer's `invoke` will see.
- A port with a custom translation gets its own wire color in the editor (issue 245) — visible signal that this port doesn't use the default path.

**Why:** The default native/JSON contract covers the cases JSON's structure handles cleanly. Everything outside that needs a user escape hatch — *and* the same hatch is useful for purely-local "I want this input shaped differently" cases. One mechanism, many uses.

**How to apply:** When the design needs an override knob on the cross-language path, propose "custom translation spec on the destination port" rather than inventing per-function annotations, type discriminator metadata, or producer-side conversion hints (all three were rejected — see feedback_cross_language_pitfalls). The port is the unit of override; the spec is user code; the rest stays default.

```

### project_editor_visual_language.md

```markdown
---
name: Editor visual language for wires
description: Wire color in the canvas editor encodes language pairing across the edge, not just routing kind — a UI invariant future work should respect (issue 245)
type: project
originSessionId: 1228a221-1c09-4c26-94f3-172d7777b2b9
---
**Wires are colored by language pairing across the edge**, with a separate color for wires whose destination port carries a user-written custom translation (issue 246). Pre-245 wires were colored purely by routing kind (default slate-blue, comparator branches orange/blue/green).

**The pairing-based palette:**
- **Same-language hop** — the wire carries native bytes; default neutral color.
- **Cross-language hop (forest green, ~`#228b22`)** — different producer/consumer languages; runtime translates via JSON at the wire. The color is a standing reminder that encode + decode is happening on that hop.
- **Custom-translation hop** — a third distinct color signaling the destination port has a user-written translation spec overriding the default path.
- Branch-routing colors (orange/blue/green from comparator boxes) compose on top — routing kind and language pairing are orthogonal axes the editor must show simultaneously.

**Why:** The runtime treats same-lang and cross-lang hops very differently (dual-ring slot design, issue 312), but pre-245 the editor gave the box author no visual signal that the JSON translation step existed. It was invisible until someone read the dispatch code. Translation cost should be legible from the canvas.

**How to apply:** Any future editor work that touches wire rendering must preserve "color encodes language pairing." Don't propose recoloring schemes that overload the cross-language signal onto another concept. New routing concepts get new axes (line style, glyphs, animation) rather than stealing color. When discussing wire visuals, name both axes — routing kind AND language pairing — even if only one is changing.

```

### project_encapsulated_map.md

```markdown
---
name: Encapsulated map as box
description: A sub-map opens as a single box on a parent canvas. Data boxes marked externally-supplied become input ports; write boxes marked externally-consumed become output ports. The documented exception to single-output-per-box (issue 248, amended 2026-05-23)
type: project
originSessionId: 1228a221-1c09-4c26-94f3-172d7777b2b9
---
**A map directory has two ways to be opened:** as the top-level graph (existing map-select UI), or as an **encapsulated box** on the currently-edited parent map (issue 248). Opening through the file browser while a parent map is open instantiates the sub-map as a single box on the parent's canvas.

**The encapsulation contract — symmetric input and output toggles:**
- **Inputs:** any `data` box inside the sub-map can be toggled "externally-supplied" in its inspector. Each such box becomes a typed input port on the encapsulated box.
- **Outputs:** any `write` box inside the sub-map can be toggled "externally-consumed" in its inspector. Each such box surfaces as a **distinct output port** on the encapsulated box. A write box with the toggle on can still write to its `path` if one is set — the file destination and the parent-wire destination are independent.

**Encapsulated maps are the documented exception to issue 218's "single output per box" rule.** They have *N* output ports — one per externally-consumed write box, where *N* may be zero (pure side-effect map), one, or many. Every other box kind still has exactly one output.

**Rejected earlier design (2026-05-21):** packing all write-box outputs into a single byte-blob with a leading null-terminated size index, single wire out. Rejected 2026-05-23 because it would have repurposed file-writing machinery to emit strings into wires — substituting one mechanism for another. The symmetric toggle design preserves the `write` box's "terminate a value" semantics and treats the destination kind as a mode toggle.

**Why:** Encapsulation is a function-like abstraction over a sub-graph. The parent doesn't see the sub-map's internals — it sees declared input ports, declared output ports, and known semantics. Sub-maps can be edited independently, reused across parents, and packaged once for distribution. This is the closest SoraMech gets to "user-defined functions" while staying graph-native.

**How to apply:** When discussing map composition, reuse, or "how do users build libraries of behavior" — encapsulation is the answer. Don't propose alternative function-abstraction mechanisms (named subgraphs as text references, macro-style inlining, blob-packed outputs) without explicitly weighing them against the symmetric-toggle shape. Both toggles (`externally-supplied` on data boxes, `externally-consumed` on write boxes) are the user-facing port-declaration mechanism — don't invent a separate "ports manifest" file.

```

### project_soramech.md

```markdown
---
name: SoraMech project context
description: Core architecture decisions, file layout, and execution model for SoraMech, across phase 2 (current) and phase 3 (designed)
type: project
originSessionId: 579d58b3-7452-44f3-983a-80ff5821082a
---
SoraMech is a visual FSM/dataflow editor and execution engine at /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/.

**Why:** Primary use case is LLM orchestration on the SoraMind Alpine cluster. Strings flow between boxes like variables. History is explicit, loaded from disk only when a box needs it.

**Three independent programs sharing a map directory:**
- `src/006-server-main.lua` — thin HTTP file CRUD proxy. No runner management.
- Runner — phase 2: `src/007-runner-main.lua` (synchronous Lua). Phase 3: `soramech-pool` (C binary, designed in issues 301–311, not yet built).
- `assets/index.html` — vanilla JS canvas editor. Editor only — no run button. Compile button (issue 222) is a phase 2 placeholder; it triggers the phase 3 build pipeline (issue 219).

**Key architecture decisions (current truth):**
- Each box is its own JSON file in `boxes/<id>.json`. Connections written to BOTH endpoint files; loader validates both ends agree — hard error if not.
- **Single output wire per box** (issue 218 complete). No `outputs` array. The function returns one value; routing is by `from_branch` on the connection.
- **Comparator-based branching** (issue 210) — replaces the earlier `branch` box kind with named ports. A box may carry a `comparand`; the dispatch layer fires `lt`/`eq`/`gt` connections. No spec is invoked for comparators.
- **Iterator boxes** (issue 221) — pure routing primitives, no `ref`/`fn`. Dispatch layer cycles through `iterator_outputs` based on a per-task counter.
- **Phase 2 drivers** are shell scripts under `drivers/` (one process per call, stdout as channel). **Phase 3 language specs** are `.so` libraries under `langs/<name>/spec.so` loaded via `dlopen` (issue 303). Lua and C are in-process; Bash is out-of-process via Unix domain socket.
- Data files: per-map JSON in `data/`. Section-level `constant` flag. Accessed via `libs/soramech-data.lua`.
- State/ephemeral: `tmp/` is a symlink to `/tmp/<map-name>/`. Phase 2 writes `last-run.json` (single doc). Phase 3 writes `last-run.jsonl` (JSON Lines, issue 311) — decoupled from the editor, consumed by LLMs / CLI viewers / test harness.
- **Phase 3 runtime model**: C pool runner owns a 3d-rts task pool; per-task slots in process heap (issue 302) with refcounting and a separate large-value heap; dispatch layer (304) is the worker action; graph loader is C (305), no embedded Lua at the runner level. Phase 2's synchronous executor retires when phase 3 lands.

**How to apply:** When working on any component, respect the three-way separation. Server never knows about runner. Runner never knows about server. Map directory format is the only shared contract. The phase 2 driver model and the phase 3 spec model coexist conceptually (phase 2 is current, phase 3 is fully designed) but the runtime mechanism differs entirely; box JSON and the editor are unchanged across the cutover.

```

### project_threading.md

```markdown
---
name: threading_model
description: SoraMech's threading model — phase 2 synchronous, phase 3 SoraMech-owned C thread pool with per-port slots and language specs
type: project
originSessionId: 579d58b3-7452-44f3-983a-80ff5821082a
---
**Phase 2 (current)**: synchronous executor in `src/004-executor.lua`. Single-threaded, sequential box execution. One driver shell-out per box call. The phase 2 path goes away when phase 3 lands — no fallback, no coroutine intermediate.

**Phase 3 (designed, not yet built)**: C pool runner with a SoraMech-owned thread pool. The 3d-rts pool at `/home/ritz/programming/ai-stuff/games/3d-rts/libs/900-task-pool.h` is the **design reference**, not vendored — SoraMech owns its pool implementation so the spawn primitive, worker context, init barrier, and iterator-pinning extensions are all under our control. The architecture is fully specified across issues 301–313.

Key phase 3 concepts:
- **Per-input-port slots** (not per-task), allocated at graph load and durable for the run. Each slot is a ring buffer (`n_cells` cells of `cell_capacity` bytes). 1-cell slots use peek-not-drain (literals, single-push wires); N-cell slots use pop-and-drain (queued / multi-push wires). No per-slot refcounting — slot lifetime is the whole run.
- **Tasks are ephemeral**: spawned dynamically by the dispatch layer when a box's input set is ready. Tasks carry just `box_id` and a counter snapshot. Box state (counter, input slots, language handle) is durable.
- **Language specs** under `langs/<name>/spec.so`, loaded via `dlopen` at startup. Uniform `lang_spec_t`. No language is privileged.
- **Same-language fast path** (issue 312): wire format defaults to JSON, but two adjacent same-language boxes use the language's native serialization (Lua `lua_dump`, C struct memcpy). Compile-time wire classification picks the path.
- **In-process specs** (Lua, C) via `lua_pcall` / `dlsym`. **Out-of-process specs** (Bash) via Unix domain sockets to a persistent per-worker subprocess.
- **Dispatch layer** (304) is the worker-side action: pop inputs → branch on box mode → invoke spec → push to downstream input slots → end. No blocking, no wait lists; tasks spawn on input arrival and run to completion.
- **Iterator and comparator routing** in the dispatch layer. Comparator runs the function and routes by output value. Iterator skips the function and routes by per-box counter (atomic spawn-time increment). Iterator tasks parallelize across workers; ordering preserved via per-push counter tags on consumer slots.
- **No-null rule** (issue 230): every input port must be wired, given a literal, or marked optional. Compile fails on missing inputs.
- **Init barrier**: pool startup waits until every worker has run every spec's `init(worker_idx)` before submitting tasks. Avoids tasks landing on uninitialized handles.
- **Hard-crash error policy**: nonzero return from any spec aborts the program.
- **Blocking semantics**: long-running operations tie up a worker. Pool size is the budget. Cooperative split (kickoff + check-done iterator) avoids holding a worker. See `docs/004-ipc-and-threading.md`.

**Effil is explicitly NOT used.** Coroutines are not used. The C pool is the chosen mechanism. 3d-rts is referenced as design inspiration, not vendored.

**How to apply:** When discussing threading, parallelism, or cross-language IPC: phase 2 is synchronous shell-out drivers; phase 3 is the SoraMech-owned C pool runner with `lang_spec_t`, per-port slot stores, and dispatch-layer routing. Never suggest effil. 3d-rts is a reference, not a dependency. Issues 301–313 carry the design.

```

### reference_strategems.md

```markdown
---
name: Strategems directory
description: Cross-cutting design rules the user has crystallized into named files at strategems/ — re-list and consider once per context
type: reference
originSessionId: 1228a221-1c09-4c26-94f3-172d7777b2b9
---
**Location:** `/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/strategems/`

**What it is:** Files in this directory are **distilled design rules** the user has named after a pattern that recurs in many places. They're shorter than docs/, longer than a one-line CLAUDE.md instruction, and load-bearing for design judgment. Each file is one strategem — a rule with a memorable phrase as the filename. The phrase-as-filename is the strategem's name; use it as shared vocabulary.

**Current strategems (as of 2026-05-23):**
- `commit-language-is-runtime-language.md`
- `contracts-must-fit-every-implementer.md`
- `redesigns-change-the-surface-too.md`

**How to apply (the user's preference is encouragement, not force):**
- **Re-list the directory once per context** (`ls strategems/`) so the current set is in my working memory. Don't auto-read each one — let the filenames sit there as triggers.
- When a filename *feels* relevant to the task at hand, read it before proceeding. Relevance signals: I'm about to propose a contract, a redesign, a language-spec change, or anything where a recurring principle might apply. If nothing feels relevant, don't force a reach.
- Re-list at session start (or first time the project surfaces) — new strategems land here without ceremony and the list above may be stale across sessions.
- When a strategem does apply, name it explicitly to the user using its filename phrase. That's the shared vocabulary the naming is for.
- If during a session the user expresses a rule in the form "X must always Y because Z" and the rule generalizes beyond the current task, suggest writing it as a strategem rather than only as a memory.

**Recent commit pattern:** strategem files are committed individually (e.g. `c57c719 strategems: contracts must fit every implementer, not just the simplest`), so `git log -- strategems/` is the fastest way to see what's been added lately and the reasoning that prompted it.

```

8	`boxes/*.json`, `data/*.json`, and metadata, decodes JSON via dkjson,
9	runs schema validation, builds the in-memory graph table, and detects
10	entry boxes. It is Lua. The synchronous executor consumes its output.
11	
12	The phase 3 pool runner (issue 301) cannot embed a Lua state for
13	loading — Lua appears in the runner only as a per-box language runtime,
14	never as the runner's own scripting layer. The loader is therefore
15	ported to C and lives inside (or alongside) `src/008-pool-runner.c`.
16	
17	## Concept
18	
19	The C graph loader is the first phase of the pool runner's startup. It:
20	1. Walks the map directory.
21	2. Parses every JSON file.
22	3. Validates schema and connection topology.
23	4. Builds a single `graph_t` struct that the pool runner and dispatch
24	   layer consume.
25	
26	The loader is a one-shot at startup. It crashes the program on any
27	validation failure (per the hard-crash policy in issue 303). There is
28	no recovery, no skip-bad-boxes mode.
29	
30	## Inputs
31	
32	The map directory layout (unchanged from phase 2):
33	
34	```
35	maps/<name>/
36	    meta.json          ← entry box, src_dirs, run-level metadata
37	    boxes/*.json       ← one file per box
38	    data/*.json        ← static data values (literal inputs)
39	    src/*              ← box function source files (resolved via meta.src_dirs)
40	```
41	
42	The loader is given the map directory path as a C string.
43	
44	## Output: `graph_t`
45	
46	```c
47	typedef struct {
48	    int           n_boxes;
49	    box_t        *boxes;            // array of box descriptors
50	    int           n_connections;
51	    connection_t *connections;
52	    int           n_size_classes;
53	    int          *size_classes;     // distinct slot sizes the graph uses
54	    int           n_entry_boxes;
55	    int          *entry_box_ids;    // indices into boxes[]
56	    int           n_languages;
57	    const char  **languages;        // distinct language names used
58	} graph_t;
59	
60	typedef struct {
61	    char         *id;               // user-defined box id ("classifier", "stamp", …)
62	    char         *kind;             // "call" | "data"
63	    char         *lang;             // "lua" | "c" | "bash" | ...; NULL for data and iterator/comparator
64	    char         *ref;               // file path; NULL for data/iterator/comparator
65	    char         *fn;                // function name; NULL for data/iterator/comparator
66	    int           n_inputs;
67	    input_decl_t *inputs;
68	    int           output_capacity;  // bytes; 0 if variable-size (uses large-value heap)
69	    int           is_comparator;
70	    int           is_iterator;
71	    int           n_iter_outputs;
72	    char        **iter_output_names;
73	    // …
74	} box_t;
75	
76	typedef struct {
77	    int   from_box_id;
78	    char *from_branch;              // NULL for plain call; "lt"/"eq"/"gt" for comparator;
79	                                    // iterator output name otherwise
80	    int   to_box_id;
81	    char *to_input_name;
82	    int   to_input_index;
83	} connection_t;
84	```
85	
86	Strings are owned by the graph (allocated from a single arena attached
87	to the graph; freed when the graph is destroyed at the end of the run).
88	
89	## Loading phases
90	
91	### 1. Directory walk
92	Open the map directory, enumerate `boxes/`, `data/`, and the optional
93	`meta.json`. Each entry is queued for parsing.
94	
95	### 2. JSON parse
96	Use the vendored JSON parser (issue 301 lists cJSON / jsmn / hand-rolled
97	as candidates). Each file becomes a parsed JSON tree. Parse errors are
98	fatal — print the file path and the parser's error message, abort.
99	
100	### 3. Schema validation
101	Box kinds and what they mean at runtime:
102	
103	- **`call`** is a verb — a box that runs a function (or, for iterator
104	  and comparator variants, a dispatch-layer routing primitive). Each
105	  call box becomes a `dispatch_task_t` in the pool when its inputs
106	  are ready.
107	- **`data`** is a noun — a literal value embedded in the map. It has
108	  no function and produces no task in the pool. Its output slot is
109	  allocated and filled at startup by the loader and remains filled
110	  for the duration of the run; downstream consumers read it like any
111	  other slot.
112	
113	Required fields per kind:
114	- `id`, `kind` always required.
115	- For `kind == "call"`: either (a) `lang`, `ref`, `fn` (plain call
116	  box), or (b) `iterator_outputs` (iterator box, no `ref`/`fn`), or
117	  (c) `comparator` field set (comparator box, no `ref`/`fn`).
118	- For `kind == "data"`: a `value` field; no `inputs` or `connections`
119	  (other than outgoing wires from this box's output slot).
120	
121	Box ids must be unique across the map. Connection endpoints must
122	reference valid box ids and valid input names.
123	
124	Schema rules mirror `src/001-schema.lua`. The C validator is a port of
125	that file's logic, simplified by the absence of port-named branching
126	(removed in issue 108 cleanup).
127	
128	### 4. Topology validation
129	- Every connection's `from_box` and `to_box` exist.
130	- Every connection's `to_input_name` matches a declared input on the
131	  destination box.
132	- No non-iterator cycles. The validator runs DFS; any cycle that does
133	  not pass through an iterator box is rejected.
134	- Every required input has at least one connection feeding it (or is
135	  fed by a `data` box).
136	
137	Validation failure aborts the program with a precise error message —
138	which box, which connection, what was wrong.
139	
140	### 5. Slot size class enumeration
141	Walk every box and queued-input declaration to enumerate the distinct
142	slot sizes the graph will need. Each unique size becomes a size class
143	in the slot allocator (issue 302). Output:
144	
145	```c
146	graph->size_classes      = { 64, 256, 1024, 65536 };  // example
147	graph->n_size_classes    = 4;
148	```
149	
150	The slot allocator pre-populates each class's free list with
151	`n_workers × (count of boxes whose output is this size)` slots. This
152	covers the worst case — every worker concurrently producing an output
153	at this size class — without overshooting. Demand beyond this triggers
154	growth (issue 302); the per-thread multiplier is the right ceiling for
155	the static count, while leaving headroom for iterators that allocate
156	per-invocation slots.
157	
158	### 6. Entry-box detection
159	A box is an entry box if either:
160	- It has zero inputs, or
161	- All of its inputs are connected only to `data` boxes (literal values
162	  with no upstream computation).
163	
164	Entry boxes are submitted to the pool first; everything else fires from
165	post-action routing in the dispatch layer.
166	
167	A map can have any number of entry boxes. The user lays out the
168	initial program state via `data` boxes and entry-point call boxes; the
169	runner submits all entries at once, and the graph propagates from
170	there. There is no singular `main`.
171	
172	### 7. Language enumeration
173	Collect the distinct `lang` values across all plain call boxes. The
174	pool runner uses this set to decide which language specs (issue 303)
175	to load and which per-worker handles to initialize.
176	
177	Iterator and comparator boxes have no `lang` and contribute nothing
178	here.
179	
180	### Note on built-in primitives vs shipped libraries
181	
182	There are two distinct categories of "things the user gets out of the
183	box," and they live in different places:
184	
185	- **Dispatch-layer primitives** are graph-topology operations whose
186	  semantics are routing, not data transformation. Iterator and
187	  comparator are the two so far. They have no `lang` / `ref` / `fn` —
188	  the dispatch layer implements their behavior directly. Splitter
189	  / fan-out is already free via wire fan-out and does not need its
190	  own box kind. Possible future primitives: a "gate" (forward only
191	  when a control input is truthy), a "merge" (forward whichever
192	  input arrives first). These are not on the roadmap; flagged here
193	  so the door stays open.
194	- **Shipped libraries** are data-transformation utilities — JSON
195	  parse/serialize, file read/write, string concat/split, regex,
196	  shell-out. These are language-specific (string semantics differ
197	  between Lua, C, and Bash) and ship as `libs/<name>.<ext>` per
198	  language. They are not built into the dispatch layer; they are
199	  ordinary call-box source files that users wire into their maps.
200	  `libs/ollama.lua` is an existing example.
201	
202	The distinction matters: dispatch-layer primitives change the graph
203	model; shipped libraries don't. A dispatch primitive can do things a
204	function call cannot (like routing on a counter); a library is just
205	code that happens to be vendored.
206	
207	## Error policy
208	
209	Any failure in any phase aborts the program. The loader prints:
210	- The map path
211	- The phase that failed (parse / schema / topology / etc.)
212	- The specific file or box that caused the failure
213	- The parser's or validator's diagnostic message
214	
215	Then `exit(1)`. There is no partial-load mode.
216	
217	## Open questions
218	
219	- JSON library choice: cJSON (heaviest, easiest, well-maintained),
220	  jsmn (tiny, no allocations, manual tree walking), or hand-rolled
221	  (smallest dependency footprint, more code). Decision deferred to
222	  implementation time.
223	### String storage
224	
225	A "string arena" is a single `malloc`-backed buffer the graph loader
226	uses to store every string it owns (box ids, file paths, function
227	names, branch tags) packed end-to-end. Each string is a `char *`
228	pointing into the arena. Allocating a string is just bumping the
229	arena's offset; freeing happens once when the arena is destroyed at
230	the end of the run, in a single `free(arena_buffer)`.
231	
232	This is faster and simpler than calling `malloc` per string: one
233	`free` for thousands of strings, no fragmentation, and lookup is
234	just a pointer.
235	
236	The arena starts small and grows on demand by `realloc`-ing to a
237	larger size when the current buffer fills up. Because every stored
238	string is referenced by a `char *` into the arena, a `realloc` that
239	moves the buffer would invalidate all the pointers — so the loader
240	either uses a series of fixed-size chunks (a chunk list rather than a
241	single growable buffer) or pre-sizes the arena from the file count
242	to avoid mid-load realloc. Chunk-list is the standard answer.
243	
244	## Suggested implementation sequence
245	
246	1. Vendor the chosen JSON parser into `libs/json/`.
247	2. Define `graph_t`, `box_t`, `connection_t`, `input_decl_t` in
248	   `src/008-pool-runner.c` (or a sibling header).
249	3. Implement phase 1 (directory walk) and phase 2 (parse). Smoke test:
250	   load `maps/hello`, print the parsed tree.
251	4. Implement phase 3 (schema validation) one rule at a time. Each
252	   rule has a unit test against a malformed map fixture.
253	5. Implement phase 4 (topology validation), including the cycle
254	   detector. Test against `maps/branch-test` (valid) and a fixture
255	   with a non-iterator cycle (invalid).
256	6. Implement phase 5 (size class enumeration). Output should match
257	   the box outputs declared in `maps/hello` and `maps/classify-demo`.
258	7. Implement phases 6 (entry boxes) and 7 (languages). Verify against
259	   known maps.
260	8. Wire the loader into `src/008-pool-runner.c`'s `main` so the pool
261	   runner starts by loading and validating before doing anything
262	   else.
263	
264	## Relevant files
265	
266	- `src/003-loader.lua` — phase 2 loader, ported by this issue
267	- `src/001-schema.lua` — phase 2 schema validator, also ported
268	- `src/008-pool-runner.c` — pool runner that owns and uses the loader
269	- `issues/301-pool-lifecycle-and-worker-init.md` — pool lifecycle that
270	  consumes the graph
271	- `issues/302-wire-value-slot-store.md` — slot allocator that consumes
272	  the size class list
273	- `issues/303-language-runtime-spec.md` — spec registry that consumes
274	  the language list
275	- `issues/304-task-dispatch-layer.md` — dispatch layer that consumes
276	  the box and connection arrays
277	- `issues/219-map-compiler.md` — the compiler is the other consumer of
278	  the same validation logic; the C loader and the compiler should
279	  share validation code if possible
280	
281	## Implementation log
282	
283	### Phase 1 + 2 + early phase 3 — 2026-05-12
284	
285	What shipped:
286	- `src/010-graph-loader.h` — public types and API. `graph_t` is
287	  opaque; the box / connection / input declaration shapes are
288	  public so the dispatch layer (issue 304) can walk them directly.
289	  Routing kinds align with issue 233 — `routing_t` with `kind`
290	  plus per-kind parameters, no legacy `comparand` /
291	  `iterator_outputs` top-level fields.
292	- `src/010-graph-loader.c` — implementation. Pipeline: open the
293	  map dir, parse `meta.json` via the project JSON parser
294	  (issue 314), walk every `.json` under `boxes/`, parse each into
295	  a `box_t`, enforce kind-specific required fields (ref/routing
296	  for call; path for data; inputs only for file_write), validate
297	  id uniqueness across boxes. Errors return a malloc'd
298	  `<file>:<line>: <message>` diagnostic via the `**err`
299	  out-parameter; the pool runner's main is expected to print and
300	  abort.
301	- `tests/maps/hello/` and `tests/maps/branching/` — fixture maps
302	  exercising the call/data combo, comparator routing (lt/eq/gt
303	  branches all present), and iterator routing (n_outputs=3).
304	- `tests/010-graph-loader-test.c` — 7 unit tests covering
305	  successful loads of both fixtures, missing map directory,
306	  NULL argument, unknown box kind, missing routing, and
307	  duplicate box id. Malformed-shape tests use mkdtemp scratch
308	  dirs so the tracked fixtures stay valid.
309	- Memory model: one `json_arena_t` per loaded graph holds every
310	  string and every parsed tree from every file; the box,
311	  connection, and input declaration arrays are plain malloc.
312	  `graph_destroy` releases all of it.
313	- `.gitignore` anchored to `/maps/` so `tests/maps/` is tracked.
314	- Makefile per-test dependency line for `010-graph-loader-test`
315	  linking the loader and the JSON parser.
316	
317	Verified `make STRICT=1` builds cleanly; 33 tests across three
318	suites (slot store 12, graph loader 7, JSON 14) all pass.
319	
320	What's deferred to follow-on iterations within 305:
321	- **Phase 4 — topology validation.** Connection endpoints store
322	  string refs only. The next pass resolves `to_box` to integer
323	  indices, verifies endpoints exist, and detects non-iterator
324	  cycles via DFS.
325	- **Phase 5 — slot size class enumeration.** The loader needs to
326	  walk every box output and produce the distinct `cell_capacity`
327	  values the slot store's allocator pre-populates.
328	- **Phase 6 — entry-box detection.** A box with zero inputs or
329	  with all inputs fed only by `data` boxes is an entry. The pool
330	  runner spawns initial tasks for these.
331	- **Phase 7 — language enumeration.** Distinct `lang` values
332	  across call boxes, handed to the spec registry (issue 303).
333	- The remaining routing kinds from issue 233 (randomizer,
334	  weighted, distributor) — the parser currently rejects them with
335	  a precise message.
336	
337	None of those block 301 / 303 / 306. Topology and language
338	enumeration land before 304 (the dispatch layer) since the
339	dispatch action needs both.
340	
341	### Topology endpoint resolution — 2026-05-12
342	
343	After `load_boxes` succeeds, a `resolve_topology` pass walks every
344	outgoing connection on every box and resolves the `to_box` string
345	to its index in `graph->boxes`, and `to_input` to its index in
346	the target box's `inputs[]`. Both string refs are preserved on
347	`connection_t` for diagnostics. Misses surface as
348	`box 'X' has a connection to nonexistent box 'Y'` or
349	`... nonexistent input 'Z'`. Two new fixture-based failure tests
350	plus an assertion on the resolved indices in the happy-path
351	`load_hello` test. The `branching` fixture grew three more box
352	files (`small`, `exact`, `big`) so its three comparator branches
353	have real targets now.
354	
355	Still deferred within 305: slot size class enumeration, entry-box
356	detection, language enumeration.
357	
358	### Non-iterator cycle detection — 2026-05-12
359	
360	Standard DFS with white/gray/black coloring; the only twist is
361	that we skip outgoing edges from iterator-routing boxes, since
362	cycles passing through an iterator are legitimate (the iterator's
363	input queue eventually empties and the loop terminates). Two new
364	fixture-based tests cover the reject case (`a → b → a`, no
365	iterator) and the allowed case (`a → iter → b → a`).
366	
367	### Runtime attach (slot allocation + spec resolution) — 2026-05-12
368	
369	`graph_attach_runtime(g, slots, specs, default_cell_bytes, err)`
370	walks every box and:
371	- allocates one 1-cell peek slot per declared input port via
372	  `slot_alloc`, storing the slot id in `box->input_slot_ids[i]`;
373	- resolves the spec for each call box by looking up `box->lang`
374	  in the registry (or, as a fallback, by matching the file
375	  extension of `box->ref`), storing the result on
376	  `box->spec_idx`.
377	
378	The graph also remembers its `map_dir` so the dispatch layer can
379	resolve relative paths for data boxes and file_write boxes.
380	
381	What's still deferred:
382	- **Language enumeration on the graph.** We resolve per-box but
383	  don't expose the distinct set of languages a map uses, so
384	  workers init every spec rather than only the ones in use.
385	
386	### Per-input slot modes + multi-spawn propagation — 2026-05-12
387	
388	`graph_attach_runtime` now picks the slot mode per input port:
389	- A new BFS pass (`propagate_multi_spawn`) seeds every iterator-
390	  routing call box as `multi_spawn = 1` and propagates the flag
391	  forward through the connection graph. Every box reachable from
392	  an iterator is now flagged.
393	- For multi-spawn boxes, each input slot is allocated as a
394	  16-cell pop ring. For single-spawn boxes, the legacy 1-cell
395	  peek slot is used.
396	- `box->input_slot_modes[]` carries the per-port mode
397	  (`SLOT_MODE_PEEK` / `SLOT_MODE_POP`) so the dispatch action
398	  picks the right read op.
399	
400	This is the compile-time wire classification the architecture
401	doc described, with a single-step forward propagation rule. A
402	proper per-wire classification (an iterator-rooted wire is
403	multi-push regardless of the immediate consumer's status) would
404	allow finer-grained mode picks; the current rule is conservative
405	in the right direction (slots that *could* be POP are POP).
406	
```

123	     * (issue 307). Lua / Bash boxes leave these at NULL / 0. All
124	     * point into the graph's arena; no separate ownership. */
125	    const char    *cflags;        /* extra flags appended to `cc`   */
126	    int            n_link_libs;
127	    const char   **link_libs;     /* each becomes `-l<name>`        */
128	    int            n_headers;
129	    const char   **headers;       /* `#include`d by generated wrappers */
130	
131	    /* Read boxes */
132	    const char    *path;          /* file path (read only; ignored if value set) */
133	    const char    *value;         /* inline literal; if set, read emits this directly */
134	
135	    /* Common */
136	    int            n_inputs;
137	    input_decl_t  *inputs;
138	    int            n_connections;
139	    connection_t  *connections;
140	
141	    /* Runtime state — populated by graph_attach_runtime, left at
142	     * defaults (-1 / NULL / 0) by graph_load alone. */
143	    int            spec_idx;       /* index into the spec registry; -1 if no spec  */
144	    int           *input_slot_ids; /* n_inputs entries; -1 if not allocated yet    */
145	    int           *input_slot_modes;/* 0 = PEEK (1-cell), 1 = POP (N-cell)         */
146	    int            counter_slot_id;/* atomic-counter slot for iterator routing; -1 */
147	    int            multi_spawn;    /* 1 if the box may fire many times in one run  */
148	
149	    /* 312 per-edge fast-path classification.
150	     *
151	     * One bit per input port: true iff the producer feeding that
152	     * port shares this box's language. When set, the dispatch
153	     * passes the slot's bytes to invoke_native as-is; when cleared
154	     * the dispatch calls json_to_native first.
155	     *
156	     * One bit per outgoing connection (not per output port — a
157	     * single output may fan to consumers of different languages):
158	     * true iff the consumer on the other end of that connection
159	     * shares this box's language. When set, push native bytes
160	     * directly; when cleared, call native_to_json first and push
161	     * JSON. */
162	    int           *input_edge_native;   /* n_inputs entries, or NULL              */
163	    int           *output_edge_native;  /* n_connections entries, or NULL         */
164	} box_t;
165	
166	/* Slot mode constants used by box_t.input_slot_modes[]. */
167	#define SLOT_MODE_PEEK 0
168	#define SLOT_MODE_POP  1
169	/* }}} */
170	
171	/* {{{ Graph */
172	typedef struct graph graph_t;
173	
174	/* Read a map directory and build the graph. Returns NULL on any
175	 * failure and sets *err to a malloc'd "<file>:<line>: <message>"
176	 * diagnostic. The caller frees *err. */
177	graph_t *graph_load(const char *map_dir, char **err);
178	
179	/* Free the graph and every byte it owns. Safe on NULL. */
180	void     graph_destroy(graph_t *g);
181	
182	/* Accessors. */
183	const char  *graph_name        (const graph_t *g);
184	const char  *graph_description (const graph_t *g);
185	const char  *graph_entry_box_id(const graph_t *g);
186	const char  *graph_map_dir     (const graph_t *g);
187	int          graph_n_boxes     (const graph_t *g);
188	const box_t *graph_box         (const graph_t *g, int i);
189	const box_t *graph_box_by_id   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);
190	
191	/* Find a box's index by id; -1 if missing. */
192	int          graph_box_index   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);
193	
194	/* Distinct language names used by call boxes in the map (filled
195	 * in by graph_attach_runtime). The pool's spec init can use this
196	 * to skip workers for specs the map doesn't actually use. */
197	int          graph_n_languages (const graph_t *g);
198	const char  *graph_language    (const graph_t *g, int i);
199	/* }}} */
200	
201	/* {{{ Runtime attach (phase 5 + 7)
202	 *
203	 * Allocates one slot per input port for every box and resolves each
204	 * call box's language spec from the registry. After this call, the
205	 * dispatch layer can read inputs via `box->input_slot_ids[i]` and
206	 * invoke the spec via `spec_registry_at(r, box->spec_idx)`.
207	 *
208	 * Slot defaults for this iteration:
209	 *   - cell_capacity = `default_cell_bytes` (or 4096 if 0)
210	 *   - n_cells = 1 (peek mode)
211	 *   - flags = 0
212	 *
213	 * Iterator-fed wires and the multi-cell pop mode land in a follow-on
214	 * — they need compile-time wire classification (issue 305 phase 5
215	 * proper) that this iteration ducks. Returns 0 on success, -1 with
216	 * *err set on the first resolution or allocation failure. */
217	struct slot_store;        /* opaque forward decl */
218	struct spec_registry;     /* opaque forward decl */
219	int graph_attach_runtime(graph_t *g,
220	                         struct slot_store    *slots,
221	                         struct spec_registry *specs,
222	                         int                   default_cell_bytes,
223	                         char                **err);
224	/* }}} */
225	
226	#ifdef __cplusplus
227	}
228	#endif
229	
230	#endif /* SORAMECH_GRAPH_LOADER_H */

ts: 2026-05-22T18:17:03.683Z

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Issue 305 ports the Lua graph loader to C because the phase-3 pool runner can't embed Lua as its own scripting layer — Lua appears only as a per-box language runtime. The loader is a one-shot at startup with hard-crash semantics, no partial-load.
The header file's "What's deferred" doc-block (lines 31–40) is actually out-of-date — the implementation log shows topology + cycle detection + attach-runtime already landed. So the comment lies. Whatever we work on, we should sync that block.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

From the implementation log, here's what's still **deferred** within 305:

1. **Phase 5 — slot size class enumeration** (proper, not the current `default_cell_bytes` shortcut). Walk every box output, collect distinct `output_capacity` values, hand them to the slot allocator.
2. **Phase 6 — entry-box detection** as a graph-level array. Currently there's only a single `graph_entry_box_id`; the issue calls for "any number of entry boxes" — zero-input boxes or boxes fed only by `read`/data boxes.
3. **Phase 7 — language enumeration on the graph.** Header has the accessor (`graph_n_languages`/`graph_language`) but the body of `graph_attach_runtime` resolves per-box without ever populating the distinct list.
4. **Remaining routing kinds:** parse `randomizer`, `weighted`, `distributor` (currently rejected with a precise message).
5. **Per-wire iterator-rooted classification** — the current multi-spawn BFS is conservative; a finer per-wire pass would let more wires stay 1-cell PEEK.
6. **Stale doc comment** in `010-graph-loader.h` lines 26–40 — "what's implemented now" is wrong.

Of these, my ordered suggestions for what to tackle:

1. **Phase 6 + 7 together** — small, complementary, both walk the box array once at the end of `graph_load` / `graph_attach_runtime`, and both unblock real consumers (pool runner submitting all entry boxes; spec init skipping unused languages). Best value-per-line.
2. **Phase 5 proper** — enumerate distinct `output_capacity` values into a `size_classes[]` on the graph; let the slot store pre-size its free lists per class instead of one `default_cell_bytes`.
3. **Routing kinds (randomizer/weighted/distributor)** — parser-only work, the dispatch layer is the actual consumer (issue 304), so this is mostly speculative until dispatch needs them.
4. **Per-wire classification refinement** — most lateral, most "design" rather than "build."

Which of these would you like to take on? (And do you want to drive any of the design decisions — e.g. should entry-box detection treat a box fed only by `read` boxes as an entry, even if some of those reads point at files that don't exist yet?)

80	        return -1;

89	        return -1;

98	        return 0;

104	        if (!c) {

113	            const char *s = json_string_value(c);

136	            return -1;

138	        return 0;

140	    if (strcmp(kind, "randomizer") == 0) {

142	        json_node_t *n = json_object_get(r, "n_outputs");
143	        if (!n || json_kind(n) != JSON_NUMBER) {
144	            *err = err_fmt("box '%s': randomizer routing missing 'n_outputs'", box_id);
145	            return -1;
146	        }
147	        out->n_outputs = (int)json_number_value(n);

150	            return -1;

152	        return 0;
153	    }
154	    if (strcmp(kind, "weighted") == 0) {
155	        out->kind = ROUTING_WEIGHTED;
156	        json_node_t *w = json_object_get(r, "weights");
157	        if (!w || json_kind(w) != JSON_ARRAY) {
158	            *err = err_fmt("box '%s': weighted routing missing 'weights' array", box_id);
159	            return -1;
160	        }
161	        int n = json_array_size(w);
162	        if (n < 1) {
163	            *err = err_fmt("box '%s': weighted routing 'weights' must be non-empty", box_id);
164	            return -1;
165	        }
166	        out->n_outputs = n;
167	        /* The graph loader's arena is not exposed to this function;
168	         * stash a heap-allocated copy and recover it in graph_destroy
169	         * via a per-box free pass — wait, simpler: allocate via the
170	         * arena. We'll use the JSON arena owned by the graph. The
171	         * routing_t already holds a const pointer; cast away const
172	         * at allocation time. */
173	        /* Use plain malloc for simplicity; the per-box free in

184	        double *vals = malloc((size_t)n * sizeof(double));

203

213	static int parse_inputs(json_node_t *node, box_t *box,
214	                        const char *box_id, char **err)
215	{
216	    if (!node) {
217	        box->n_inputs = 0;
218	        box->inputs = NULL;
219	        return 0;
220	    }
221	    if (json_kind(node) != JSON_ARRAY) {
222	        *err = err_fmt("box '%s': 'inputs' is not an array", box_id);
223	        return -1;
224	    }
225	    int n = json_array_size(node);
226	    box->n_inputs = n;
227	    if (n == 0) { box->inputs = NULL; return 0; }
228	
229	    box->inputs = calloc((size_t)n, sizeof(input_decl_t));
230	    if (!box->inputs) {
231	        *err = err_fmt("out of memory");
232	        return -1;
233	    }
234	
235	    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
236	        json_node_t *item = json_array_at(node, i);
237	        if (!item || json_kind(item) != JSON_OBJECT) {
238	            *err = err_fmt("box '%s': input port %d is not an object", box_id, i);
239	            return -1;
240	        }
241	        json_node_t *name_n = json_object_get(item, "name");
242	        if (!name_n || json_kind(name_n) != JSON_STRING) {
243	            *err = err_fmt("box '%s': input port %d missing 'name'", box_id, i);
244	            return -1;
245	        }
246	        box->inputs[i].name = json_string_value(name_n);
247	
248	        json_node_t *type_n = json_object_get(item, "type");
249	        box->inputs[i].type = (type_n && json_kind(type_n) == JSON_STRING)
250	                                ? json_string_value(type_n) : "string";
251	
252	        /* `value`: only string literals supported in this iteration. */
253	        json_node_t *val_n = json_object_get(item, "value");
254	        box->inputs[i].literal = (val_n && json_kind(val_n) == JSON_STRING)
255	                                    ? json_string_value(val_n) : NULL;
256	
257	        json_node_t *opt_n = json_object_get(item, "optional");
258	        box->inputs[i].optional = (opt_n && json_kind(opt_n) == JSON_BOOL)
259	                                    ? json_bool_value(opt_n) : 0;
260	    }
261	    return 0;
262	}

267	                             const char *box_id, char **err)
268	{
269	    if (!node) {
270	        box->n_connections = 0;
271	        box->connections = NULL;

306

309	         * iterator. The validation that the branch matches the
310	         * routing kind lives in phase 4 (topology). */
311	        json_node_t *fb = json_object_get(c, "from_branch");
