319	/* {{{ parse_box_file() — read one boxes/<id>.json into a box_t */
320	static int parse_box_file(graph_t *g, box_t *box,
321	                          const char *path, char **err)
322	{
323	    int err_line = 0;
324	    const char *err_msg = NULL;
325	    json_node_t *n = json_parse_file(g->arena, path, &err_line, &err_msg);
326	    if (!n) {
327	        *err = err_fmt("%s:%d: %s", path, err_line, err_msg ? err_msg : "parse error");
328	        return -1;
329	    }
330	    if (json_kind(n) != JSON_OBJECT) {
331	        *err = err_fmt("%s: top-level must be a JSON object", path);
332	        return -1;
333	    }
334	
335	    memset(box, 0, sizeof *box);
336	    box->spec_idx          = -1;   /* unresolved until graph_attach_runtime */
337	    box->counter_slot_id   = -1;   /* only iterator boxes allocate one      */
338	    box->multi_spawn       = 0;    /* set by graph_attach_runtime           */
339	
340	    /* id */
341	    json_node_t *id_n = json_object_get(n, "id");
342	    if (!id_n || json_kind(id_n) != JSON_STRING) {
343	        *err = err_fmt("%s: missing or non-string 'id'", path);
344	        return -1;
345	    }
346	    box->id = json_string_value(id_n);
347	
348	    /* kind */
349	    json_node_t *kind_n = json_object_get(n, "kind");
350	    if (!kind_n || json_kind(kind_n) != JSON_STRING) {
351	        *err = err_fmt("%s: box '%s' missing 'kind'", path, box->id);
352	        return -1;
353	    }
354	    const char *kind_str = json_string_value(kind_n);
355	    if      (strcmp(kind_str, "call")  == 0) box->kind = BOX_CALL;
356	    else if (strcmp(kind_str, "read")  == 0) box->kind = BOX_READ;
357	    else if (strcmp(kind_str, "write") == 0) box->kind = BOX_WRITE;
358	    else {
359	        *err = err_fmt("%s: box '%s': unknown kind '%s'", path, box->id, kind_str);
360	        return -1;
361	    }
362	
363	    /* Kind-specific required fields */
364	    if (box->kind == BOX_CALL) {
365	        json_node_t *ref_n = json_object_get(n, "ref");
366	        if (!ref_n || json_kind(ref_n) != JSON_STRING) {
367	            *err = err_fmt("%s: call box '%s' missing 'ref'", path, box->id);
368	            return -1;
369	        }
370	        box->ref = json_string_value(ref_n);
371	
372	        json_node_t *fn_n = json_object_get(n, "fn");
373	        if (fn_n && json_kind(fn_n) == JSON_STRING) {
374	            box->fn = json_string_value(fn_n);
375	        }
376	
377	        json_node_t *lang_n = json_object_get(n, "lang");
378	        if (lang_n && json_kind(lang_n) == JSON_STRING) {
379	            box->lang = json_string_value(lang_n);
380	        }
381	
382	        json_node_t *r_n = json_object_get(n, "routing");
383	        if (parse_routing(r_n, &box->routing, box->id, err) != 0) return -1;
384	
385	        json_node_t *oc_n = json_object_get(n, "output_capacity");
386	        if (oc_n && json_kind(oc_n) == JSON_NUMBER) {
387	            box->output_capacity = (int)json_number_value(oc_n);
388	        }
389	
390	        /* Declared return type. Optional; absence means "raw bytes,
391	         * format up to the spec." Specs that care (the C spec's
392	         * JSON-output branch, for example) read this on the
393	         * output_native=0 path. */
394	        json_node_t *ret_n = json_object_get(n, "returns");
395	        if (ret_n && json_kind(ret_n) == JSON_STRING) {
396	            box->returns = json_string_value(ret_n);
397	        }
398	
399	        /* Per-box compile hints (issue 307 items 3+4). All optional;
400	         * any spec that ignores them gets the default behaviour. The
401	         * arena owns the strings and arrays — graph_destroy doesn't
402	         * free them. */
403	        json_node_t *cf_n = json_object_get(n, "cflags");
404	        if (cf_n && json_kind(cf_n) == JSON_STRING) {
405	            box->cflags = json_string_value(cf_n);
406	        }
407	        /* The pointer array is malloc'd (and freed in graph_destroy);
408	         * the string contents are owned by the JSON arena and stay
409	         * alive for the run. Same pattern as the routing weights
410	         * array elsewhere in this file. */
411	        json_node_t *ll_n = json_object_get(n, "link_libs");
412	        if (ll_n && json_kind(ll_n) == JSON_ARRAY) {
413	            int sz = json_array_size(ll_n);
414	            if (sz > 0) {
415	                const char **arr = malloc((size_t)sz * sizeof(*arr));
416	                if (!arr) {
417	                    *err = err_fmt("%s: oom for link_libs", box->id);
418	                    return -1;
419	                }
420	                for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) {
421	                    json_node_t *e = json_array_at(ll_n, i);
422	                    if (!e || json_kind(e) != JSON_STRING) {
423	                        free(arr);
424	                        *err = err_fmt("%s: link_libs[%d] is not a string",
425	                                       box->id, i);
426	                        return -1;
427	                    }
428	                    arr[i] = json_string_value(e);
429	                }
430	                box->link_libs   = arr;
431	                box->n_link_libs = sz;
432	            }
433	        }
434	        json_node_t *hd_n = json_object_get(n, "headers");
435	        if (hd_n && json_kind(hd_n) == JSON_ARRAY) {
436	            int sz = json_array_size(hd_n);
437	            if (sz > 0) {
438	                const char **arr = malloc((size_t)sz * sizeof(*arr));
439	                if (!arr) {
440	                    *err = err_fmt("%s: oom for headers", box->id);
441	                    return -1;
442	                }
443	                for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) {
444	                    json_node_t *e = json_array_at(hd_n, i);
445	                    if (!e || json_kind(e) != JSON_STRING) {
446	                        free(arr);
447	                        *err = err_fmt("%s: headers[%d] is not a string",
448	                                       box->id, i);
449	                        return -1;
450	                    }
451	                    arr[i] = json_string_value(e);
452	                }
453	                box->headers   = arr;
454	                box->n_headers = sz;
455	            }
456	        }
457	    } else if (box->kind == BOX_READ) {
458	        /* read accepts EITHER an inline `value` literal OR a `path`
459	         * field. value takes precedence at run time (the canvas
460	         * hides the path input port when value is set, per issue
461	         * 229). When neither is set, the box may still be valid if
462	         * its `path` arrives at runtime via the input wire — that's
463	         * checked in dispatch, not here. */
464	        json_node_t *val_n  = json_object_get(n, "value");
465	        json_node_t *path_n = json_object_get(n, "path");
466	        if (val_n && json_kind(val_n) == JSON_STRING) {
467	            box->value = json_string_value(val_n);
468	        }
469	        if (path_n && json_kind(path_n) == JSON_STRING) {
470	            box->path = json_string_value(path_n);
471	        }
472	    }
473	    /* write boxes: nothing additional beyond inputs. */
474	
475	    if (parse_inputs(json_object_get(n, "inputs"),
476	                     box, box->id, err) != 0) return -1;
477	    if (parse_connections(json_object_get(n, "connections"),
478	                          box, box->id, err) != 0) return -1;
479	
480	    return 0;
481	}
482	/* }}} */
483	
484	/* {{{ count_json_files_in_dir() — first pass, count .json entries */
485	static int count_json_files_in_dir(const char *dir, char **err)
486	{
487	    DIR *d = opendir(dir);
488	    if (!d) {
489	        *err = err_fmt("cannot open directory '%s'", dir);
490	        return -1;
491	    }
492	    int n = 0;
493	    struct dirent *e;
494	    while ((e = readdir(d))) {
495	        if (e->d_name[0] == '.') continue;
496	        if (ends_with(e->d_name, ".json")) n++;
497	    }
498	    closedir(d);
499	    return n;
500	}
501	/* }}} */
502	
503	/* {{{ load_meta() — parse map_dir/meta.json into the graph */
504	static int load_meta(graph_t *g, const char *map_dir, char **err)
505	{
506	    char path[4096];
507	    int w = snprintf(path, sizeof path, "%s/meta.json", map_dir);
508	    if (w < 0 || w >= (int)sizeof path) {
509	        *err = err_fmt("meta.json path too long");
510	        return -1;
511	    }
512	
513	    int err_line = 0; const char *err_msg = NULL;
514	    json_node_t *meta = json_parse_file(g->arena, path, &err_line, &err_msg);
515	    if (!meta) {
516	        *err = err_fmt("%s:%d: %s", path, err_line, err_msg ? err_msg : "parse error");
517	        return -1;
518	    }
519	    if (json_kind(meta) != JSON_OBJECT) {
520	        *err = err_fmt("%s: top-level must be a JSON object", path);
521	        return -1;
522	    }
523	
524	    json_node_t *name = json_object_get(meta, "name");
525	    if (!name || json_kind(name) != JSON_STRING) {
526	        *err = err_fmt("%s: missing 'name'", path);
527	        return -1;
528	    }
529	    g->name = json_string_value(name);
530	
531	    json_node_t *entry = json_object_get(meta, "entry_box_id");
532	    if (entry && json_kind(entry) == JSON_STRING) {
533	        g->entry_box_id = json_string_value(entry);
534	    }
535	
536	    json_node_t *desc = json_object_get(meta, "description");
537	    if (desc && json_kind(desc) == JSON_STRING) {
538	        g->description = json_string_value(desc);
539	    }
540	
541	    return 0;
542	}
543	/* }}} */
544	
545	/* {{{ load_boxes() — walk boxes/, parse every .json into g->boxes */
546	static int load_boxes(graph_t *g, const char *map_dir, char **err)
547	{
548	    char dir[4096];
549	    int w = snprintf(dir, sizeof dir, "%s/boxes", map_dir);
550	    if (w < 0 || w >= (int)sizeof dir) {
551	        *err = err_fmt("boxes path too long");
552	        return -1;
553	    }
554	
555	    int n = count_json_files_in_dir(dir, err);
556	    if (n < 0) return -1;
557	
558	    g->n_boxes = n;
559	    if (n == 0) { g->boxes = NULL; return 0; }
560	    g->boxes = calloc((size_t)n, sizeof(box_t));
561	    if (!g->boxes) { *err = err_fmt("out of memory"); return -1; }
562	
563	    DIR *d = opendir(dir);
564	    if (!d) { *err = err_fmt("cannot open directory '%s'", dir); return -1; }
565	
566	    int i = 0;
567	    struct dirent *e;
568	    while ((e = readdir(d))) {
569	        if (e->d_name[0] == '.')              continue;
570	        if (!ends_with(e->d_name, ".json"))   continue;
571	
572	        char path[4096];
573	        int wp = snprintf(path, sizeof path, "%s/%s", dir, e->d_name);
574	        if (wp < 0 || wp >= (int)sizeof path) {
575	            closedir(d);
576	            *err = err_fmt("box path too long");
577	            return -1;
578	        }
579	
580	        if (parse_box_file(g, &g->boxes[i], path, err) != 0) {
581	            closedir(d);
582	            return -1;
583	        }
584	        i++;
585	    }
586	    closedir(d);
587	
588	    /* Validate id uniqueness (O(n^2) scan; n is small). */
589	    for (int j = 0; j < n; j++) {
590	        for (int k = j + 1; k < n; k++) {
591	            if (strcmp(g->boxes[j].id, g->boxes[k].id) == 0) {
592	                *err = err_fmt("duplicate box id '%s'", g->boxes[j].id);
593	                return -1;
594	            }
595	        }
596	    }
597	
598	    return 0;
599	}
600	/* }}} */
601	
602	/* {{{ box_is_iterator() — true iff this is a call box whose routing
603	 * kind cuts cycles. Cycles passing through an iterator are
604	 * legitimate (the iterator's input queue eventually empties and
605	 * the cycle terminates); cycles that don't are deadlocks at
606	 * load time and must be rejected. */
607	static int box_is_iterator(const box_t *b)
608	{
609	    return b->kind == BOX_CALL && b->routing.kind == ROUTING_ITERATOR;
610	}
611	/* }}} */
612	
613	/* {{{ dfs_cycle() — recursive DFS for non-iterator cycle detection */
614	/* colors: 0 = white (not seen), 1 = gray (on current stack),
615	 * 2 = black (finished). Returns 0 on success, -1 if a cycle was
616	 * found; *culprit_box is the box where the back-edge was detected. */
617	static int dfs_cycle(const graph_t *g, int box_id, char *color,
618	                     int *culprit_box)
619	{
620	    color[box_id] = 1;
621	    const box_t *src = &g->boxes[box_id];
622	
623	    /* Skip outgoing edges from iterator boxes. The iterator naturally
624	     * terminates on input-queue empty, so cycles that loop through
625	     * one are legal. Skipping is the cleanest way to express
626	     * "this edge doesn't propagate cycle reachability." */
627	    if (!box_is_iterator(src)) {
628	        for (int i = 0; i < src->n_connections; i++) {
629	            int dst = src->connections[i].to_box_idx;
630	            if (color[dst] == 1) { *culprit_box = box_id; return -1; }
631	            if (color[dst] == 0) {
632	                if (dfs_cycle(g, dst, color, culprit_box) != 0) return -1;
633	            }
634	        }
635	    }
636	    color[box_id] = 2;
637	    return 0;
638	}
639	/* }}} */
640	
641	/* {{{ detect_cycles() — phase 4 part 2 */
642	static int detect_cycles(const graph_t *g, char **err)
643	{
644	    if (g->n_boxes <= 0) return 0;
645	    char *color = calloc((size_t)g->n_boxes, 1);
646	    if (!color) { *err = err_fmt("out of memory"); return -1; }
647	
648	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
649	        if (color[i] != 0) continue;
650	        int culprit = -1;
651	        if (dfs_cycle(g, i, color, &culprit) != 0) {
652	            *err = err_fmt("non-iterator cycle detected (back-edge from "
653	                           "box '%s'); cycles must pass through an "
654	                           "iterator-routing box",
655	                           g->boxes[culprit].id);
656	            free(color);
657	            return -1;
658	        }
659	    }
660	    free(color);
661	    return 0;
662	}
663	/* }}} */
664	
665	/* {{{ resolve_topology() — phase 4 (partial: endpoints only) */
666	/* Walks every outgoing connection on every box. Looks up `to_box`
667	 * as a box id and `to_input` as one of that box's declared input
668	 * port names; fills in the integer indices. Errors out with a
669	 * precise message on either miss. Cycle detection lands in a
670	 * later iteration. */
671	static int resolve_topology(graph_t *g, char **err)
672	{
673	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
674	        box_t *src = &g->boxes[i];
675	        for (int j = 0; j < src->n_connections; j++) {
676	            connection_t *c = &src->connections[j];
677	            c->to_box_idx   = -1;
678	            c->to_input_idx = -1;
679	
680	            int found_box = -1;
681	            for (int k = 0; k < g->n_boxes; k++) {
682	                if (strcmp(g->boxes[k].id, c->to_box) == 0) { found_box = k; break; }
683	            }
684	            if (found_box < 0) {
685	                *err = err_fmt("box '%s' has a connection to nonexistent box '%s'",
686	                               src->id, c->to_box);
687	                return -1;
688	            }
689	            c->to_box_idx = found_box;
690	
691	            const box_t *dst = &g->boxes[found_box];
692	            int found_in = -1;
693	            for (int k = 0; k < dst->n_inputs; k++) {
694	                if (strcmp(dst->inputs[k].name, c->to_input) == 0) {
695	                    found_in = k; break;
696	                }
697	            }
698	            if (found_in < 0) {
699	                *err = err_fmt("box '%s' has a connection to box '%s' on "
700	                               "nonexistent input '%s'",
701	                               src->id, dst->id, c->to_input);
702	                return -1;
703	            }
704	            c->to_input_idx = found_in;
705	        }
706	    }
707	    return 0;
708	}
709	/* }}} */
710	
711	/* {{{ graph_load() */
712	graph_t *graph_load(const char *map_dir, char **err)
713	{
714	    if (err) *err = NULL;
715	    if (!map_dir) {
716	        if (err) *err = err_fmt("graph_load: NULL map_dir");
717	        return NULL;
718	    }
719	
720	    graph_t *g = calloc(1, sizeof *g);
721	    if (!g) { if (err) *err = err_fmt("out of memory"); return NULL; }
722	
723	    g->arena = json_arena_create();
724	    if (!g->arena) {
725	        if (err) *err = err_fmt("out of memory");
726	        free(g);
727	        return NULL;
728	    }
729	
730	    g->map_dir = strdup(map_dir);
731	    if (!g->map_dir) {
732	        if (err) *err = err_fmt("out of memory");
733	        graph_destroy(g);
734	        return NULL;
735	    }
736	
737	    if (load_meta(g, map_dir, err)     != 0) { graph_destroy(g); return NULL; }
738	    if (load_boxes(g, map_dir, err)    != 0) { graph_destroy(g); return NULL; }
739	    if (resolve_topology(g, err)       != 0) { graph_destroy(g); return NULL; }
740	    if (detect_cycles(g, err)          != 0) { graph_destroy(g); return NULL; }
741	
742	    /* Compute the same-language fast path flag per call box (issue
743	     * 312). A box is "native-eligible" iff every adjacent call box —
744	     * any producer feeding its inputs AND any consumer reading its
745	     * output — shares the box's language. Read boxes and
746	     * write boxes don't constrain (they emit/consume JSON-shaped
747	     * bytes that any spec interprets through its bridges).
748	     *
749	     * Computed in graph_load (not graph_attach_runtime) because
750	     * it's purely topology-derived and doesn't need the slot
751	     * store or registry. The dispatch action picks
752	     * `spec->invoke_native` when this flag is set, falling back to
753	     * `invoke_json` or `invoke`. Our current specs all leave
754	     * invoke_native NULL so the fallback runs and behaviour is
755	     * unchanged — but the infrastructure is in place. */
756	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
757	        box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];
758	        b->input_edge_native  = NULL;
759	        b->output_edge_native = NULL;
760	        if (b->kind != BOX_CALL || !b->lang) continue;
761	
762	        /* Allocate per-port input bits and per-connection output
763	         * bits. malloc rather than calloc — we fill every entry
764	         * explicitly below. */
765	        if (b->n_inputs > 0) {
766	            b->input_edge_native = malloc((size_t)b->n_inputs * sizeof(int));
767	            if (!b->input_edge_native) { if (err) *err = err_fmt("oom"); return NULL; }
768	            for (int j = 0; j < b->n_inputs; j++) b->input_edge_native[j] = 0;
769	        }
770	        if (b->n_connections > 0) {
771	            b->output_edge_native = malloc((size_t)b->n_connections * sizeof(int));
772	            if (!b->output_edge_native) { if (err) *err = err_fmt("oom"); return NULL; }
773	            for (int j = 0; j < b->n_connections; j++) b->output_edge_native[j] = 0;
774	        }
775	
776	        /* Outgoing edges: for each connection, the consumer's lang
777	         * is native iff it's also a call box in this box's
778	         * language. Read / write consumers are not native
779	         * (they don't carry a language). */
780	        int all_outs_native = 1;
781	        for (int j = 0; j < b->n_connections; j++) {
782	            int dst = b->connections[j].to_box_idx;
783	            if (dst < 0) { all_outs_native = 0; continue; }
784	            const box_t *c = &g->boxes[dst];
785	            int native = (c->kind == BOX_CALL && c->lang
786	                          && strcmp(c->lang, b->lang) == 0);
787	            b->output_edge_native[j] = native;
788	            if (!native) all_outs_native = 0;
789	        }
790	
791	        (void)all_outs_native;   /* slice 5 of 312 removed the per-box bit */
792	
793	        /* Incoming edges: for each input port, scan every producer
794	         * in the graph; the port is native iff at least one
795	         * producer feeds it AND every feeding producer shares this
796	         * box's language. (A mixed-language fan-in downgrades the
797	         * port to JSON for the whole port — the slot can carry only
798	         * one format, and JSON is the common denominator.) */
799	        for (int port = 0; port < b->n_inputs; port++) {
800	            int any_producer = 0;
801	            int all_native   = 1;
802	            for (int p = 0; p < g->n_boxes; p++) {
803	                const box_t *prod = &g->boxes[p];
804	                for (int k = 0; k < prod->n_connections; k++) {
805	                    if (prod->connections[k].to_box_idx != i)        continue;
806	                    if (prod->connections[k].to_input_idx != port)   continue;
807	                    any_producer = 1;
808	                    int native = (prod->kind == BOX_CALL && prod->lang
809	                                  && strcmp(prod->lang, b->lang) == 0);
810	                    if (!native) all_native = 0;
811	                }
812	            }
813	            b->input_edge_native[port] = (any_producer && all_native) ? 1 : 0;
814	        }
815	    }
816	    return g;
817	}
818	/* }}} */
819	
820	/* {{{ graph_destroy() */
821	void graph_destroy(graph_t *g)
822	{
823	    if (!g) return;
824	    if (g->boxes) {
825	        for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
826	            free(g->boxes[i].inputs);
827	            free(g->boxes[i].connections);
828	            free(g->boxes[i].input_slot_ids);
829	            free(g->boxes[i].input_slot_modes);
830	            free(g->boxes[i].input_edge_native);
831	            free(g->boxes[i].output_edge_native);
832	            free((double *)g->boxes[i].routing.weights);
833	            /* per-box compile hint pointer arrays (strings inside
834	             * are arena-owned; only the array itself is heap) */
835	            free((void *)g->boxes[i].link_libs);
836	            free((void *)g->boxes[i].headers);
837	        }
838	        free(g->boxes);
839	    }
840	    free(g->languages);    /* element strings live in the arena */
841	    free(g->map_dir);
842	    json_arena_destroy(g->arena);
843	    free(g);
844	}
845	/* }}} */
846	
847	/* {{{ Accessors */
848	const char *graph_name(const graph_t *g)         { return g ? g->name : NULL; }
849	const char *graph_description(const graph_t *g)  { return g ? g->description : NULL; }
850	const char *graph_entry_box_id(const graph_t *g) { return g ? g->entry_box_id : NULL; }
851	const char *graph_map_dir(const graph_t *g)      { return g ? g->map_dir : NULL; }
852	int         graph_n_boxes(const graph_t *g)      { return g ? g->n_boxes : 0; }
853	
854	const box_t *graph_box(const graph_t *g, int i)
855	{
856	    if (!g || i < 0 || i >= g->n_boxes) return NULL;
857	    return &g->boxes[i];
858	}
859	
860	const box_t *graph_box_by_id(const graph_t *g, const char *id)
861	{
862	    if (!g || !id) return NULL;
863	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
864	        if (strcmp(g->boxes[i].id, id) == 0) return &g->boxes[i];
865	    }
866	    return NULL;
867	}
868	
869	int graph_box_index(const graph_t *g, const char *id)
870	{
871	    if (!g || !id) return -1;
872	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
873	        if (strcmp(g->boxes[i].id, id) == 0) return i;
874	    }
875	    return -1;
876	}
877	
878	int graph_n_languages(const graph_t *g) { return g ? g->n_languages : 0; }
879	
880	const char *graph_language(const graph_t *g, int i)
881	{
882	    if (!g || i < 0 || i >= g->n_languages) return NULL;
883	    return g->languages[i];
884	}
885	/* }}} */
886	
887	/* {{{ resolve_spec_for_box() — find the spec index by box->lang */
888	/* Returns the spec's index in the registry, or -1 if not found. */
889	static int resolve_spec_for_box(const box_t *box, spec_registry_t *r)
890	{
891	    if (!box || !r) return -1;
892	    if (box->kind != BOX_CALL) return -1;
893	    if (box->lang) {
894	        for (int i = 0; i < spec_registry_size(r); i++) {
895	            const lang_spec_t *s = spec_registry_at(r, i);
896	            if (strcmp(s->name, box->lang) == 0) return i;
897	        }
898	    }
899	    /* Fallback: derive from ref extension if lang isn't explicit.
900	     * Simple search for the last '.' and match against file_ext. */
901	    if (box->ref) {
902	        const char *dot = strrchr(box->ref, '.');
903	        if (dot) {
904	            for (int i = 0; i < spec_registry_size(r); i++) {
905	                const lang_spec_t *s = spec_registry_at(r, i);
906	                if (s->file_ext && strcmp(s->file_ext, dot) == 0) return i;
907	            }
908	        }
909	    }
910	    return -1;
911	}
912	/* }}} */
913	
914	/* {{{ propagate_multi_spawn() — BFS forward from iterators */
915	/* A box is "multi_spawn" if it's an iterator OR if any producer
916	 * feeding one of its inputs is itself multi_spawn. The marker
917	 * propagates forward through the connection graph. */
918	#define MULTI_SPAWN_RING_CELLS 16
919	
920	static void propagate_multi_spawn(graph_t *g)
921	{
922	    /* Seed: every iterator-routing call box is multi_spawn. */
923	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
924	        box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];
925	        b->multi_spawn = (b->kind == BOX_CALL &&
926	                          b->routing.kind == ROUTING_ITERATOR) ? 1 : 0;
927	    }
928	    /* Fixed-point: as long as any new box gets marked, iterate. The
929	     * graph is small (≤ a few hundred boxes in practice), so the
930	     * O(n × edges) bound is fine. */
931	    int changed = 1;
932	    while (changed) {
933	        changed = 0;
934	        for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
935	            const box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];
936	            if (!b->multi_spawn) continue;
937	            for (int j = 0; j < b->n_connections; j++) {
938	                int dst = b->connections[j].to_box_idx;
939	                if (dst < 0) continue;
940	                if (!g->boxes[dst].multi_spawn) {
941	                    g->boxes[dst].multi_spawn = 1;
942	                    changed = 1;
943	                }
944	            }
945	        }
946	    }
947	}
948	/* }}} */
949	
950	/* {{{ graph_attach_runtime() — phase 5 + 7 */
951	int graph_attach_runtime(graph_t *g,
952	                         struct slot_store *slots,
953	                         struct spec_registry *specs,
954	                         int default_cell_bytes,
955	                         char **err)
956	{
957	    if (!g || !slots) {
958	        if (err) *err = err_fmt("graph_attach_runtime: NULL graph or slots");
959	        return -1;
960	    }
961	    if (default_cell_bytes <= 0) default_cell_bytes = 4096;
962	
963	    /* Mark multi-spawn boxes via forward BFS from iterators so we
964	     * can pick the right slot mode for each input port below. */
965	    propagate_multi_spawn(g);
966	
967	    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
968	        box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];
969	
970	        /* Allocate slots per input port. Multi-spawn boxes get
971	         * N-cell pop rings so producers' multiple pushes accumulate
972	         * and each task drains one cell. Single-spawn boxes get
973	         * 1-cell peek slots (the legacy fast path). */
974	        if (b->n_inputs > 0) {
975	            b->input_slot_ids   = malloc((size_t)b->n_inputs * sizeof(int));
976	            b->input_slot_modes = malloc((size_t)b->n_inputs * sizeof(int));
977	            if (!b->input_slot_ids || !b->input_slot_modes) {
978	                if (err) *err = err_fmt("out of memory allocating input slots");
979	                return -1;
980	            }
981	            int n_cells = b->multi_spawn ? MULTI_SPAWN_RING_CELLS : 1;
982	            int mode    = b->multi_spawn ? SLOT_MODE_POP : SLOT_MODE_PEEK;
983	            /* Multi-spawn rings tag each cell so pops serve in
984	             * producer-supplied order (issue 304). Iterators pass
985	             * their counter as the tag; non-iterator producers pass
986	             * 0 and effectively get FIFO ordering. */
987	            int base_flags = b->multi_spawn ? SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED : 0;
988	            for (int j = 0; j < b->n_inputs; j++) {
989	                /* If any producer feeding this input port declares
990	                 * variable-size output (output_capacity == 0), the
991	                 * slot needs the large-value heap path so pushes
992	                 * larger than default_cell_bytes succeed. Read boxes
993	                 * default to output_capacity 0 since they emit
994	                 * arbitrary file contents; a call box opts in by
995	                 * setting output_capacity to 0 in its JSON. Issue
996	                 * 302 follow-on. */
997	                int slot_flags = base_flags;
998	                for (int p = 0; p < g->n_boxes && !(slot_flags & SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE); p++) {
999	                    const box_t *prod = &g->boxes[p];
1000	                    for (int k = 0; k < prod->n_connections; k++) {
1001	                        if (prod->connections[k].to_box_idx == i &&
1002	                            prod->connections[k].to_input_idx == j &&
1003	                            prod->output_capacity == 0) {
1004	                            slot_flags |= SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE;
1005	                            break;
1006	                        }
1007	                    }
1008	                }
1009	
1010	                /* Slice 3 of issue 312: call-box input ports become
1011	                 * dual-ring slots so producers can write native or
1012	                 * JSON per per-edge classification, and consumers
1013	                 * see per-cell which_ring tags. Restrictions inherited
1014	                 * from slice 1: DUAL_RING doesn't combine with
1015	                 * LARGE_VALUE or TAGGED yet, so ports requiring those
1016	                 * fall back to the single-ring path (mixed-format
1017	                 * fan-in on those ports lands in a follow-on along
1018	                 * with the LARGE_VALUE / TAGGED combinations). */
1019	                if (b->kind == BOX_CALL && b->lang &&
1020	                    !(slot_flags & (SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE | SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED))) {
1021	                    slot_flags |= SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING;
1022	                }
1023	
1024	                slot_id_t id = slot_alloc((slot_store_t *)slots,
1025	                                          default_cell_bytes, n_cells,
1026	                                          slot_flags);
1027	                if (id == SLOT_INVALID) {
1028	                    if (err) *err = err_fmt("box '%s': failed to allocate "
1029	                                            "slot for input '%s'",
1030	                                            b->id, b->inputs[j].name);
1031	                    return -1;
1032	                }
1033	                b->input_slot_ids[j]   = (int)id;
1034	                b->input_slot_modes[j] = mode;
1035	            }
1036	        }
1037	
1038	        /* Resolve the spec index for call boxes. Non-call boxes
1039	         * stay at -1; the dispatch layer handles them directly. */
1040	        b->spec_idx = -1;
1041	        if (b->kind == BOX_CALL && specs) {
1042	            b->spec_idx = resolve_spec_for_box(b, (spec_registry_t *)specs);
1043	            if (b->spec_idx < 0) {
1044	                if (err) *err = err_fmt("box '%s': no spec found for "
1045	                                        "lang='%s' / ref='%s'",
1046	                                        b->id, b->lang ? b->lang : "(null)",
1047	                                        b->ref ? b->ref : "(null)");
1048	                return -1;
1049	            }
1050	        }
1051	
1052	        /* Iterator boxes need an atomic-counter slot so the dispatch
1053	         * layer can pick the output branch via slot_read_inc with
1054	         * concurrent-safe semantics. */
1055	        b->counter_slot_id = -1;
1056	        if (b->kind == BOX_CALL &&
1057	            (b->routing.kind == ROUTING_ITERATOR   ||
1058	             b->routing.kind == ROUTING_RANDOMIZER ||
1059	             b->routing.kind == ROUTING_WEIGHTED)) {
1060	            slot_id_t cid = slot_alloc((slot_store_t *)slots, 0, 0,
1061	                                       SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER);
1062	            if (cid == SLOT_INVALID) {
1063	                if (err) *err = err_fmt("box '%s': could not allocate "
1064	                                        "atomic-counter slot", b->id);
1065	                return -1;
1066	            }
1067	            b->counter_slot_id = (int)cid;
1068	        }
1069	    }
1070	
1071	    /* Enumerate distinct languages used by call boxes. Stored as
1072	     * a small array of arena-owned string pointers; the pool init
1073	     * can use it to skip specs no box in this map uses. */
1074	    free(g->languages);
1075	    g->languages   = NULL;
1076	    g->n_languages = 0;
1077	    if (g->n_boxes > 0) {
1078	        g->languages = calloc((size_t)g->n_boxes, sizeof(const char *));
1079	        if (!g->languages) {
1080	            if (err) *err = err_fmt("out of memory");
1081	            return -1;
1082	        }
1083	        for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {
1084	            const box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];
1085	            if (b->kind != BOX_CALL || !b->lang) continue;
1086	            int seen = 0;
1087	            for (int k = 0; k < g->n_languages; k++) {
1088	                if (strcmp(g->languages[k], b->lang) == 0) { seen = 1; break; }
1089	            }
1090	            if (!seen) g->languages[g->n_languages++] = b->lang;
1091	        }
1092	    }
1093	    return 0;
1094	}
1095	/* }}} */
1096	
```

/* tests/010-graph-loader-test.c — unit tests for the graph loader.
 *
 * Exercises the phase 1 + phase 2 + early phase 3 work shipped in
 * this iteration: directory walk, JSON parse, per-box schema
 * validation. Topology / cycle / language-enumeration tests land
 * with their iterations.
 *
 * Fixtures live under tests/maps/. Tests cd to the project root so
 * relative paths work regardless of where the test binary is run.
 */

#include "010-graph-loader.h"
#include "009-slot-store.h"
#include "011-spec-registry.h"

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <sys/stat.h>
#include <unistd.h>

/* {{{ Test harness */
static int g_pass = 0;
static int g_fail = 0;

#define ASSERT(cond) \
    do { if (!(cond)) { \
        fprintf(stderr, "      %s:%d: %s\n", __FILE__, __LINE__, #cond); \
        return 0; \
    } } while (0)

#define RUN(name) \
    do { \
        fprintf(stdout, "  %-44s ", #name); fflush(stdout); \
        if (test_##name()) { fprintf(stdout, "ok\n"); g_pass++; } \
        else                { fprintf(stdout, "FAIL\n"); g_fail++; } \
    } while (0)
/* }}} */

/* {{{ Path resolution — find tests/maps/ relative to cwd */
static const char *FIXTURE_DIR = "tests/maps";

/* If tests are run from project root: tests/maps/ exists.
 * If run from build/tests/: the binary was started by make test,
 * which cd's to project root first. We don't try to be clever here. */
static int has_fixtures(void)
{
    struct stat st;
    return stat(FIXTURE_DIR, &st) == 0 && S_ISDIR(st.st_mode);
}
/* }}} */

/* {{{ test_load_hello() */
static int test_load_hello(void)
{
    char *err = NULL;
    graph_t *g = graph_load("tests/maps/hello", &err);
    if (!g) {
        fprintf(stderr, "      graph_load failed: %s\n", err ? err : "(null)");
        free(err);
        return 0;
    }

    ASSERT(strcmp(graph_name(g), "hello") == 0);
    ASSERT(strcmp(graph_entry_box_id(g), "greet") == 0);
    ASSERT(graph_description(g) != NULL);
    ASSERT(graph_n_boxes(g) == 2);

    /* greet box */
    const box_t *greet = graph_box_by_id(g, "greet");
    ASSERT(greet != NULL);
    ASSERT(greet->kind == BOX_CALL);
    ASSERT(strcmp(greet->lang, "lua") == 0);
    ASSERT(strcmp(greet->ref,  "src/hello.lua") == 0);
    ASSERT(strcmp(greet->fn,   "greet") == 0);
    ASSERT(greet->routing.kind == ROUTING_PLAIN);
    ASSERT(greet->output_capacity == 256);
    ASSERT(greet->n_inputs == 2);
    ASSERT(strcmp(greet->inputs[0].name, "name") == 0);
    ASSERT(greet->inputs[0].literal == NULL);

{
  "old_string":"### 3. Schema validation\nBox kinds and what they mean at runtime:\n\n- **`call`** is a verb — a box that runs a function (or, for iterator\n  and comparator variants, a dispatch-layer routing primitive). Each\n  call box becomes a `dispatch_task_t` in the pool when its inputs\n  are ready.\n- **`data`** is a noun — a literal value embedded in the map. It has\n  no function and produces no task in the pool. Its output slot is\n  allocated and filled at startup by the loader and remains filled\n  for the duration of the run; downstream consumers read it like any\n  other slot.\n\nRequired fields per kind:\n- `id`, `kind` always required.\n- For `kind == \"call\"`: either (a) `lang`, `ref`, `fn` (plain call\n  box), or (b) `iterator_outputs` (iterator box, no `ref`/`fn`), or\n  (c) `comparator` field set (comparator box, no `ref`/`fn`).\n- For `kind == \"data\"`: a `value` field; no `inputs` or `connections`\n  (other than outgoing wires from this box's output slot).\n\nBox ids must be unique across the map. Connection endpoints must\nreference valid box ids and valid input names.\n\nSchema rules mirror `src/001-schema.lua`. The C validator is a port of\nthat file's logic, simplified by the absence of port-named branching\n(removed in issue 108 cleanup).",
  "replace_all":false,
  "new_string":"### 3. Schema validation\nBox kinds and what they mean at runtime:\n\n- **`call`** is a verb — a box that runs a function. Each call box\n  becomes a dispatch task in the pool when its inputs are ready. The\n  branching primitives that used to be separate box kinds\n  (comparator / iterator) are now expressed as a `routing` field on a\n  call box; the dispatch layer reads `routing.kind` to pick the\n  outgoing branch.\n- **`read`** is a value source. It can carry an inline `value`\n  literal or a `path` that the dispatch layer reads at run time.\n  When `value` is set the canvas hides the `path` input port. A\n  read box has no function to invoke — it just produces a value\n  downstream consumers can read like any other slot.\n- **`write`** is a file sink. It consumes one input and emits the\n  string `\"true\"` downstream after a successful write so it can be\n  chained.\n\nRequired fields per kind:\n- `id`, `kind` always required.\n- For `kind == \"call\"`: `ref` (source file) plus a `routing` object\n  (`{ \"kind\": \"plain\" | \"comparator\" | \"iterator\" | ... }` with the\n  per-kind parameters). `lang` and `fn` are optional — `lang` falls\n  back to the file-extension lookup in the spec registry, `fn`\n  defaults to the box id at dispatch time.\n- For `kind == \"read\"`: at least one of `value` (inline literal) or\n  `path` (file). Either can also arrive at run time on the implicit\n  `path` input port — the run-time check lives in dispatch.\n- For `kind == \"write\"`: no extra fields beyond `inputs` (the input\n  carries the bytes to write and the path).\n\nBox ids must be unique across the map. Connection endpoints must\nreference valid box ids and valid input port names.",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/issues/305-c-graph-loader.md"
}

{
  "old_string":"### 6. Entry-box detection\nA box is an entry box if either:\n- It has zero inputs, or\n- All of its inputs are connected only to `data` boxes (literal values\n  with no upstream computation).\n\nEntry boxes are submitted to the pool first; everything else fires from\npost-action routing in the dispatch layer.\n\nA map can have any number of entry boxes. The user lays out the\ninitial program state via `data` boxes and entry-point call boxes; the\nrunner submits all entries at once, and the graph propagates from\nthere. There is no singular `main`.",
  "replace_all":false,
  "new_string":"### 6. Entry-box detection\nAn \"entry box\" is one the pool runner submits as an initial task. A\nbox qualifies when it is a `call` or `write` box (the kinds that\nactually run as tasks) AND either:\n- it has zero inputs, or\n- every one of its non-optional inputs is fed only by `read` boxes\n  — literal-or-file value sources with no upstream computation.\n  Inputs flagged `optional` in the schema are ignored when checking\n  this condition, so a box that has all its computed inputs satisfied\n  by literals still qualifies even if an optional port is wired to\n  another call box (it just won't fire until the optional value\n  arrives).\n\nRead boxes never qualify — they aren't tasks; their value is staged\ninto their downstream consumers' input slots at dispatch time.\n\nA map can have any number of entry boxes. The runner submits all of\nthem at once and the graph propagates from there; there is no\nsingular `main`. The single `entry_box_id` field on `meta.json`\nremains supported as a hint but is not the canonical answer.",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/issues/305-c-graph-loader.md"
}

  "old_string":"/* {{{ ends_with() — true iff `s` ends with `suffix` */\nstatic int ends_with(const char *s, const char *suffix)\n{\n    size_t ls = strlen(s), lf = strlen(suffix);\n    if (ls < lf) return 0;\n    return memcmp(s + ls - lf, suffix, lf) == 0;\n}\n/* }}} */",
  "replace_all":false,
  "new_string":"/* {{{ ends_with() — true iff `s` ends with `suffix` */\nstatic int ends_with(const char *s, const char *suffix)\n{\n    size_t ls = strlen(s), lf = strlen(suffix);\n    if (ls < lf) return 0;\n    return memcmp(s + ls - lf, suffix, lf) == 0;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ input_feeders_t / scan_input_feeders() — one reverse-scan, four facts\n *\n * Three earlier loops in this file walked all boxes looking for\n * connections ending at a given (dst_box, port) — the same scan,\n * collecting different facts. They are now one helper. dst_lang may\n * be NULL when the caller doesn't care about the native count. */\ntypedef struct {\n    int n_feeders;     /* total connections ending at (dst_box, port)        */\n    int n_native;      /* feeders that are call boxes sharing dst_lang       */\n    int n_non_read;    /* feeders that aren't BOX_READ value sources         */\n    int has_variable;  /* any feeder declares output_capacity == 0           */\n    int max_capacity;  /* max declared output_capacity across feeders (>= 0) */\n} input_feeders_t;\n\nstatic input_feeders_t scan_input_feeders(const struct graph *g,\n                                          int dst_box, int port_idx,\n                                          const char *dst_lang)\n{\n    input_feeders_t f = {0};\n    for (int p = 0; p < g->n_boxes; p++) {\n        const box_t *prod = &g->boxes[p];\n        for (int k = 0; k < prod->n_connections; k++) {\n            const connection_t *c = &prod->connections[k];\n            if (c->to_box_idx   != dst_box)  continue;\n            if (c->to_input_idx != port_idx) continue;\n            f.n_feeders++;\n            if (prod->kind != BOX_READ) f.n_non_read++;\n            if (dst_lang && prod->kind == BOX_CALL && prod->lang &&\n                strcmp(prod->lang, dst_lang) == 0) {\n                f.n_native++;\n            }\n            if (prod->output_capacity == 0) {\n                f.has_variable = 1;\n            } else if (prod->output_capacity > f.max_capacity) {\n                f.max_capacity = prod->output_capacity;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    return f;\n}\n/* }}} */",

  "replace_all":false,
  "new_string":"/* {{{ detect_entry_boxes() — phase 6\n *\n * Fills g->entry_box_ids with the indices of boxes the pool runner\n * should submit as initial tasks. A box qualifies when:\n *   - it is BOX_CALL or BOX_WRITE (the kinds that run as tasks —\n *     BOX_READ is a value source, never a task), AND\n *   - every non-optional input port has zero non-read feeders.\n *     Read boxes (literal-or-file value sources) don't count as\n *     upstream computation; a box fed only by reads can fire\n *     immediately on startup. Optional ports are ignored.\n *\n * A zero-input call box passes the loop vacuously and qualifies.\n *\n * The scan reuses scan_input_feeders so its definition of \"feeder\"\n * matches the native-edge and large-value passes — single source of\n * truth for \"what feeds (box, port)\". */\nstatic int detect_entry_boxes(graph_t *g, char **err)\n{\n    if (g->n_boxes <= 0) {\n        g->n_entry_boxes = 0;\n        g->entry_box_ids = NULL;\n        return 0;\n    }\n    int *ids = malloc((size_t)g->n_boxes * sizeof(int));\n    if (!ids) { *err = err_fmt(\"out of memory\"); return -1; }\n\n    int count = 0;\n    for (int i = 0; i < g->n_boxes; i++) {\n        const box_t *b = &g->boxes[i];\n        if (b->kind != BOX_CALL && b->kind != BOX_WRITE) continue;\n\n        int qualifies = 1;\n        for (int port = 0; port < b->n_inputs; port++) {\n            if (b->inputs[port].optional) continue;\n            input_feeders_t f = scan_input_feeders(g, i, port, NULL);\n            if (f.n_non_read > 0) { qualifies = 0; break; }\n        }\n        if (qualifies) ids[count++] = i;\n    }\n    g->n_entry_boxes = count;\n    g->entry_box_ids = (count > 0) ? ids : (free(ids), NULL);\n    return 0;\n}\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ resolve_topology() — phase 4 (partial: endpoints only) */",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/src/010-graph-loader.c"
}

611	/* {{{ test_variable_size_producer_lvh_wiring() */
612	/* 302 follow-on: graph_attach_runtime sets SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE on
613	 * any input slot whose producer declares variable-size output
614	 * (output_capacity == 0). The hello fixture pairs greet (call,
615	 * output_capacity=256) with who (data box, output_capacity defaults
616	 * to 0). After attach, greet's input port 0 (`name`, fed by who)
617	 * should accept a push that exceeds the slot's normal cell
618	 * capacity, while port 1 (`salutation`, literal-only) should not. */
619	static int test_variable_size_producer_lvh_wiring(void)
620	{
621	    char *err = NULL;
622	    graph_t *g = graph_load("tests/maps/hello", &err);
623	    ASSERT(g);
624	
625	    spec_registry_t *r = spec_registry_load("langs", &err);
626	    ASSERT(r);
627	
628	    slot_store_t *s = slot_store_create();
629	    ASSERT(s);
630	    /* Tight default cell so the discriminator (push 2 KB) is well
631	     * outside it — without LARGE_VALUE the push would fail. */
632	    ASSERT(graph_attach_runtime(g, s, r, 512, &err) == 0);
633	
634	    const box_t *greet = graph_box_by_id(g, "greet");
635	    ASSERT(greet && greet->n_inputs == 2);
636	
637	    char big[2048];
638	    memset(big, 'A', sizeof big);
639	
640	    /* Port 0 (`name`) is fed by data box `who` → LARGE_VALUE. */
641	    ASSERT(slot_push(s, greet->input_slot_ids[0], big, sizeof big, 0) == 0);
642	
643	    /* Port 1 (`salutation`) has only a literal — no variable-size
644	     * producer → fixed-size slot → 2 KB push exceeds cell capacity
645	     * and fails. */
646	    ASSERT(slot_push(s, greet->input_slot_ids[1], big, sizeof big, 0) == -1);
647	
648	    slot_store_destroy(s);
649	    spec_registry_destroy(r);
650	    graph_destroy(g);
651	    return 1;
652	}
653	/* }}} */
654	
655	/* {{{ main() */
656	int main(void)
657	{

661	                        "run from project root\n");
662	        return 2;
663	    }
664	    printf("010-graph-loader-test:\n");
665	    RUN(load_hello);

ts: 2026-05-22T18:30:36.187Z

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The "spaghetti" you flagged shows up most when three places in the file each grow their own copy of the same reverse-scan loop, and then each one starts collecting one extra fact that the other two don't know about. Folding them into a single `scan_input_feeders` that returns a struct of facts means the next time you need a new fact about "what feeds this port" you add one line to the struct, not a fourth nested loop. Same shape works for any other "scan-and-collect" that's currently spread across passes.

Notice that the helper sits one level *above* the slot store and the JSON parser — it doesn't reach into either. The loader stays the only thing that knows the topology, and downstream passes consume a small structured answer rather than re-walking the graph. That separation is what kept this refactor from cascading.

A future move worth considering: a one-shot reverse index built right after `resolve_topology` so even `scan_input_feeders` becomes O(feeders) instead of O(n_boxes × avg_connections). Not worth doing yet — graphs are small — but the seam is in the right place for when it is.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Issue 305 is fully implemented. All seven phases now run in `graph_load` + `graph_attach_runtime`:
- The phase-6 entry-box array is exposed via `graph_n_entry_boxes` / `graph_entry_box`.
- Phase-5 slot widths are picked per-port from the max declared `output_capacity` across feeders, with the distinct set exposed via `graph_n_size_classes` / `graph_size_class`.
- `distributor` routing parses cleanly and gets its counter slot.
- The three duplicated reverse-scan loops are now one helper.
- 18/18 loader tests pass and the full suite is green.

The issue file's "current behavior" section and stale header doc-block both match reality now, and a short list of further-refactor opportunities is recorded in the issue file so they're not lost. Want me to commit, or look at any of the remaining refactor seams next?

The four future-refactor items I flagged (dedup the `n_outputs` parsing, the string-array parsing, the directory double-walk, the linear id lookups) don't change what the loader *does* — they only change how cheaply or how tidily it does it. Calling those "deferred" would mean the issue could never be closed, because any code can be tidier. The right move is to mark the issue complete and let the seams either get picked up by a future refactor issue or quietly stay as-is forever, depending on whether they ever bite.

---
# 306 — Lua language spec implementation (reference spec)

## Status
open

## Current behavior
Lua boxes today run via `drivers/lua.sh`, which spawns a fresh `luajit`
process per box invocation, feeds it stdin, and reads stdout. Process
spawn cost dominates; each call is its own fresh interpreter. There is
no persistent Lua state between calls.

## Concept
The Lua language spec implements `lang_spec_t` (issue 303) for Lua. It
is the first reference implementation of the spec interface and the
proof that the in-process FFI model works. It is also the simplest
case — no compile step, no out-of-process server.

Each worker thread gets its own `lua_State`. States are not shared.
Per-thread states need no locking on the call path; the state's
internals are only ever touched by the worker that owns it.

`langs/lua/spec.c` is built into `langs/lua/spec.so` and loaded by
the pool runner via `dlopen` at startup, like any other spec.

## Per-worker state

Init creates a fresh `lua_State` and configures it:

```c
void *lua_init(int worker_idx) {
    lua_State *L = luaL_newstate();
    luaL_openlibs(L);
    set_package_path(L);   // libs/, map src/, meta.json src_dirs
    return L;
}

void lua_teardown(void *handle) {
    lua_close((lua_State *)handle);
}
```
```

```
## Implementation log

### Init + invoke + teardown — 2026-05-12

`langs/lua/spec.c` now exports a real `lang_spec_t`:

- `init(worker_idx)` creates a `lua_State` and opens the standard
  libraries via `luaL_openlibs`. Returns the state as the per-
  worker handle.
- `teardown(handle)` calls `lua_close`.
- `invoke(handle, file_path, fn_name, inputs, sizes, n, out_buf,
  out_cap, out_size)` loads the file with `luaL_loadfile`,
  executes the chunk (which is expected to return a module
  table), pulls the named function out of that table, pushes each
  input as a Lua string, calls via `lua_pcall`, and copies the
  return as a string into the caller's buffer. The state's stack
  is rewound to its baseline depth at every entry/exit so
  cross-call leaks don't accumulate. Errors at every step
  surface as nonzero return and a stderr message.

Five end-to-end tests in `tests/306-lua-spec-test.c` exercise the
init/teardown round-trip, a `greet(name, salutation)` call with
both args, the same call with one arg (the Lua side defaults
salutation to "Hello"), and the two failure modes (missing
function, missing file). The test loads the spec through the
real spec registry via dlopen, so this is the actual artifact
the pool runner will consume.

Deferred:
- **Rich return types.** The current invoke serializes the return
  with `lua_tolstring`, which handles strings/numbers/booleans/
  nil via tostring semantics. Tables, closures, and other
  structured values land with the fast-path work in issue 312.

### Per-worker module cache — 2026-05-12

`lua_init` now seeds an empty Lua table at
`LUA_REGISTRYINDEX[LUA_CACHE_KEY]`. `lua_invoke` looks up the
box's `file_path` in that table; on a hit it skips the
`luaL_loadfile + lua_pcall` round-trip and uses the cached
module table directly; on a miss it loads, runs the chunk,
verifies the module table shape, and stashes the result in the
cache before proceeding.

The cache is per-worker (each worker has its own `lua_State`),
which means no locking. It's also per-file-path string, so two
boxes that share a source file share a module table on each
worker — calling `M.greet` from one box doesn't reload the
chunk when another box later calls `M.farewell` on the same
file.

Cache invalidation is intentionally absent for now: once a file
is loaded, its module table sticks for the run. If the box
source changes mid-run, the worker won't notice. Fine for the
read-once-then-execute usage; revisit when there's a use case
for hot-reload.
```

## Implementation log (continued)

### JSON-input acceptance (primitive level) — 2026-05-21

`langs/c/spec.c` gains `try_strip_json_primitive`, called per
input port when `input_native[i] == 0`. Behavior:

- JSON string `"hello"` → user function receives the unquoted
  bytes `hello` (5 bytes, no quotes).
- JSON number / bool / null → passthrough; the JSON
  representation is already the form the C function's
  `atol` / `strncmp` / etc. expect.
- JSON arrays / objects → passthrough (raw bytes); proper
  projection needs the typed-wrapper generation that's still
  ahead in items 2-4 above.
- Parse failure → passthrough. Matches Lua's input-side
  parse-with-fallback so producers that don't yet emit real
  JSON keep working.

The C spec's `invoke` reuses a stack-allocated scratch matrix
(`scratch[C_MAX_INPUTS][4096]`) — the substituted bytes live
inside the invoke's frame so the lifetime question is trivial,
and no heap allocation happens on the call path. The C box
function still uses the same fixed signature
(`int fn(const void **, const int *, int, void *, int, int *)`);
the JSON unwrap is transparent to it.

This slice unblocks issue 312's slice 4.5 — Lua now writes JSON
for cross-language outputs, and a Lua → C wire with a primitive
payload (string or number) flows correctly end-to-end. The
pipeline integration test (`tests/maps/pipeline`) exercises this
path: Lua doubles a number, C adds one, Bash exclaims, end-to-end.

`tests/307-c-spec-test.c` gains three tests:
`invoke_json_string_input` (quotes stripped),
`invoke_json_number_input` (raw passthrough on JSON number),
`invoke_json_falls_back_on_non_json` (raw passthrough on parse
failure). `langs/c/Makefile` now folds in `libs/json/json.c`
the same way the Lua spec does.

### compile callback signature + per-box build hints — 2026-05-21

The spec interface's `compile` callback grew a third parameter,
`const box_t *box`. Calls with a real box record (compile-button
pipeline, future typed-wrapper generation) get access to the
box's identity and compile hints; the internal lazy-compile path
in `maybe_lazy_compile` passes `NULL` because it operates on a
raw `.c` ref with no box context. `langs/lang-spec.h` carries a
forward `typedef struct box box_t;` so specs that don't read the
fields don't need the loader header pulled in.

`box_t` (in `src/010-graph-loader.h`) grew four optional fields,
all parsed from the box JSON when present:

- `cflags` — a single string of additional flags appended to gcc.
  Whitespace-split into argv tokens at compile time.
- `link_libs[]` — array of library names. Each becomes a
  `-l<name>` argv entry. The pointer array is malloc'd and freed
  by `graph_destroy`; the strings inside live in the JSON arena.
- `headers[]` — array of header names. Parsed and stored; not yet
  consumed by the spec. Lights up with wrapper generation.
- `n_link_libs` / `n_headers` — counts.

`langs/c/spec.c`'s `c_compile` walks the box's `cflags` and
`link_libs` into a bounded argv (96 slots), forks gcc, and waits.
The previous fixed-flags-only execlp call is replaced by execvp
so the variable arg list can flow through. Failure modes (cflags
too long, too many tokens, argv overflow, oom) all return -1
with a stderr line naming the box id.
```

## Suggested implementation sequence

   `spec.so` exporting a stub `soramech_lang_spec`.

   `dlopen` the spec and call `init` per worker without errors.
3. Implement file-and-function caching — load a file, store its
   returned table in the registry, look it up later.
4. Implement input marshalling: push bytes as strings, count args.
5. Implement `lua_pcall` invocation with error wrapping.

   end-to-end through the pool.

Looked at both. Status:

**306 (Lua spec) — not blocked, and arguably already done.** Its implementation log records init/invoke/teardown, per-worker module cache, and (under issue 312's slice 4) full JSON input parsing + JSON output writing. The "Open questions" section is empty ("(none currently)"). The only thing the log explicitly deferred was "rich return types (tables/closures), lands with 312" — and that has since landed (the encoder in `langs/lua/spec.c` now turns Lua tables into JSON objects / arrays). I think 306 is ready to mark complete; the only work is a final verification pass + a body-update parallel to what we did for 305.

**307 (C spec) — not blocked by another issue, but has one substantive piece of unfinished work inside its own scope.** The "Remaining work" section lists four items; three are done, item 2 is still open:

> **JSON-output writing.** When `output_native == 0`, the C spec writes a JSON serialization of the return value rather than the user function's raw bytes. The wrapper knows the return type, so it can construct the JSON shape. Symmetric to (1); pending typed-wrapper generation so the spec knows what shape to serialize.

The dependency is internal — item 2 is gated on the typed-wrapper generation that the C spec hasn't built yet. The `headers` field on `box_t` is parsed but unused for the same reason ("lights up with wrapper generation"). That generator is the meat of what's left.

So my suggestion (most-valuable first):

1. **Close out 306** — verify the spec body matches what shipped, drop the deferred-rich-returns line from the log since it's untrue now, and move it to `completed/` like we just did with 305.
2. **Implement 307's typed-wrapper generation** — this is the substantive build: a small code generator that emits a per-box wrapper around the user's C function, converting native bytes ⇄ JSON per the declared input and return types. Once that's in, both item 2 (JSON output) and the dormant `headers` field light up together.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The pattern across 305/306/307 is the same: a phase-3 issue grows substantial scope, the implementation log accumulates a paragraph per slice, and by the time the work is "done" the issue body lags reality by a couple of months. The cure is the same maneuver we did on 305 — pull the "Current behavior" section forward to match what shipped, drop resolved open questions, and let the log keep its history. The issue file is a living spec, not a changelog.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Want me to start with 306 (close-out pass), then move to 307 (typed wrapper)? Or take them in a different order?

_tool_result:_
```
1	# 306 — Lua language spec implementation (reference spec)
2	
3	## Status
4	open
5	
6	## Current behavior
7	Lua boxes today run via `drivers/lua.sh`, which spawns a fresh `luajit`
8	process per box invocation, feeds it stdin, and reads stdout. Process
9	spawn cost dominates; each call is its own fresh interpreter. There is
10	no persistent Lua state between calls.
11	
12	## Concept
13	The Lua language spec implements `lang_spec_t` (issue 303) for Lua. It
14	is the first reference implementation of the spec interface and the
15	proof that the in-process FFI model works. It is also the simplest
16	case — no compile step, no out-of-process server.
17	
18	Each worker thread gets its own `lua_State`. States are not shared.
19	Per-thread states need no locking on the call path; the state's
20	internals are only ever touched by the worker that owns it.
21	
22	`langs/lua/spec.c` is built into `langs/lua/spec.so` and loaded by
23	the pool runner via `dlopen` at startup, like any other spec.
24	
25	## Per-worker state
26	
27	Init creates a fresh `lua_State` and configures it:
28	
29	```c
30	void *lua_init(int worker_idx) {
31	    lua_State *L = luaL_newstate();
32	    luaL_openlibs(L);
33	    set_package_path(L);   // libs/, map src/, meta.json src_dirs
34	    return L;
35	}
36	
37	void lua_teardown(void *handle) {
38	    lua_close((lua_State *)handle);
39	}
40	```
41	
42	`set_package_path` writes `package.path` and `package.cpath` to
43	include exactly one directory: the map's own `src/`. Everything the
44	map needs — vendored libs, helper modules, anything — is copied into
45	`src/` by the compile step (issue 222). The compiled map is then a
46	self-contained directory: ship the directory to someone else and it
47	runs without further setup.
48	
49	This is a tightening of the phase 2 path, which also included
50	SoraMech's central `libs/` and any `meta.json src_dirs`. The new rule
51	is: one place to look for functions, the map's own `src/`, full stop.
52	Compile is the step that resolves dependencies into that location.
53	
54	## File-and-function caching
55	
56	A naive `invoke` would `luaL_loadfile` and look up the function on
57	every call. Both are unnecessary work — the file's chunk does not
58	change during a run, and the function reference is stable.
59	
60	The Lua spec keeps a per-handle cache: for each box source file
61	loaded, store the table returned by the file's chunk as a Lua
62	registry key (`LUA_REGISTRYINDEX`). On first invocation:
63	1. `luaL_loadfile(L, file_path)` — loads the chunk.
64	2. `lua_pcall(L, 0, 1, 0)` — runs the chunk, expecting a returned
65	   table.
66	3. Store the table in the registry under a key derived from `file_path`.
67	
68	On subsequent invocations:
69	1. Look up the registry entry by `file_path`.
70	2. Index it by `fn_name` to get the function reference.
71	3. Skip `luaL_loadfile` entirely.
72	
73	The cache is per-`lua_State` and therefore per-worker; no
74	synchronization is needed.
75	
76	## Box file convention
77	
78	A Lua box file returns a table whose keys are the box's exported
79	function names:
80	
81	```lua
82	-- maps/<name>/src/classify.lua
83	local M = {}
84	
85	function M.classify(text)
86	    if text:match("^%d+$") then return "numeric"
87	    elseif text:match("[a-z]") then return "alpha"
88	    else return "other"
89	    end
90	end
91	
92	return M
93	```
94	
95	The box JSON references it by `ref = "classify.lua"`, `fn = "classify"`.
96	The spec loads `classify.lua`, stores its returned table, and indexes
97	into it with `"classify"` to get the function.
98	
99	This is the existing phase 2 convention — kept as-is.
100	
101	## Input marshalling
102	
103	Inputs arrive at `invoke` as `(void *bytes, int size)` pairs. The
104	spec is responsible for converting each input's bytes into the Lua
105	value type the user's function expects. The user's function sees
106	arguments as native Lua values — never a raw byte buffer that the
107	function has to parse itself.
108	
109	Each input has a declared type in the box JSON
110	(`{"name": "count", "type": "number"}`). The graph loader (issue 305)
111	records this type on the `input_decl_t` and passes the type alongside
112	the bytes when calling `invoke`. The Lua spec dispatches on type:
113	
114	| Declared type | Lua spec action                                             |
115	|---------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|
116	| `string`      | `lua_pushlstring(L, bytes, size)`                           |
117	| `number`      | parse with `strtod`, `lua_pushnumber`                       |
118	| `integer`     | parse with `strtoll`, `lua_pushinteger`                     |
119	| `boolean`     | strcmp against `"true"` / `"false"`, `lua_pushboolean`      |
120	| `json`        | call `dkjson.decode` on the bytes, push the resulting table |
121	| `bytes`       | `lua_pushlstring` (raw, may contain nulls)                  |
122	| `any`         | treat as `string` for now                                   |
123	
124	The Lua function then takes typed arguments:
125	
126	```lua
127	function M.add(a, b)            -- both already numbers
128	    return a + b
129	end
130	
131	function M.classify(payload)    -- payload is already a table
132	    return payload.kind
133	end
134	```
135	
136	The user does not write `tonumber` or `dkjson.decode` calls inside
137	their box function. The spec did that work before the function was
138	called.
139	
140	If a parse fails (`json` input that isn't valid JSON, `number` input
141	that isn't a number), the spec returns nonzero and the dispatch layer
142	aborts (per issue 303).
143	
144	## Return marshalling
145	
146	The Lua function returns one or more values. The spec converts the
147	return into bytes for the output slot:
148	
149	| Return shape           | Spec action                                                   |
150	|------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|
151	| Single string          | `lua_tolstring` → copy into `out_buf`                         |
152	| Single number          | `snprintf("%.17g", ...)` into `out_buf`                       |
153	| Single boolean         | write `"true"` or `"false"`                                   |
154	| Single table           | `dkjson.encode` into `out_buf`                                |
155	| Single nil             | `*out_size = 0` (zero-byte output)                            |
156	| Multiple values        | wrap into a table `{v1, v2, ...}` and `dkjson.encode`         |
157	
158	`nil` is a valid return. The spec writes zero bytes and sets
159	`*out_size = 0`. Downstream consumers receive a zero-byte slot value;
160	how they interpret it depends on their declared input type (typed
161	`number` consumer fails to parse; typed `string` consumer gets an
162	empty string; typed `json` consumer parses zero bytes as `null` via
163	dkjson). We cannot demand that user functions never return nil, so we
164	handle it.
165	
166	Multiple return values are wrapped into a table and JSON-encoded.
167	Phase 2 silently dropped extra returns; phase 3 keeps them — the
168	serialized form is `[v1, v2, ...]`.
169	
170	Output buffer is sized to the box's declared `output_capacity`
171	(issue 305's `box_t`). If the serialized value exceeds that, abort
172	(per the hard-crash policy in issue 303). For variable-size outputs
173	the box declares `output_capacity = 0` and uses the large-value heap
174	(issue 302); the spec writes into that heap and stores the handle in
175	`out_buf`.
176	
177	dkjson is the table serializer in both directions (input parsing
178	above and output serialization here). It is already vendored.
179	
180	## Error handling
181	
182	Every `lua_pcall` is wrapped:
183	```c
184	int rc = lua_pcall(L, n_args, 1, 0);
185	if (rc != LUA_OK) {
186	    fprintf(stderr, "[lua spec] %s:%s — %s\n",
187	            file_path, fn_name, lua_tostring(L, -1));
188	    lua_pop(L, 1);
189	    return 1;  // dispatch layer aborts the program
190	}
191	```
192	
193	Lua errors (`error(...)`, runtime errors, syntax errors during file
194	load) all surface here as nonzero `pcall` returns. The spec prints
195	the box context and the Lua error message to stderr, then returns
196	nonzero. The dispatch layer aborts.
197	
198	There is no try-and-recover. Per issue 303, errors crash the program.
199	
200	## Build
201	
202	```
203	langs/lua/
204	    spec.c          ← lang_spec_t implementation
205	    Makefile        ← builds spec.so
206	    spec.so         ← built artifact (gitignored)
207	```
208	
209	The Makefile target:
210	```make
211	spec.so: spec.c
212		$(CC) -shared -fPIC -O2 -Wall \
213		    -I$(LUA_INCLUDE) \
214		    -o $@ $< \
215		    -L$(LUA_LIB) -lluajit-5.1
216	```
217	
218	LuaJIT is the assumed runtime — it matches the phase 2 driver and the
219	project preference (LuaJIT-compatible Lua, no 5.4 syntax). The build
220	links against the system LuaJIT shared library.
221	
222	## Open questions
223	
224	(none currently — earlier questions resolved as follows:)
225	
226	- nil return: valid. Zero-byte output, downstream consumers
227	  interpret per their declared input type.
228	- Multiple return values: wrapped into a table and JSON-encoded via
229	  dkjson. No values dropped.
230	- Table serializer: dkjson, already vendored. Used in both
231	  directions (input parsing and output serialization).
232	
233	## Suggested implementation sequence
234	
235	1. Set up `langs/lua/` directory with a Makefile that builds an empty
236	   `spec.so` exporting a stub `soramech_lang_spec`.
237	2. Implement `init` and `teardown`. Verify the pool runner can
238	   `dlopen` the spec and call `init` per worker without errors.
239	3. Implement file-and-function caching — load a file, store its
240	   returned table in the registry, look it up later.
241	4. Implement input marshalling: push bytes as strings, count args.
242	5. Implement `lua_pcall` invocation with error wrapping.
243	6. Implement string return marshalling. Smoke test: `maps/hello`
244	   end-to-end through the pool.
245	7. Implement number return marshalling.
246	8. Implement table-via-JSON return marshalling.
247	9. Add `package.path` resolution for libs/, src/, and `meta.src_dirs`.
248	10. Test against `maps/classify-demo` and `maps/driver-test` (Lua
249	    portions).
250	
251	## Relevant files
252	
253	- `drivers/lua.sh` — phase 2 Lua driver, retired by this spec
254	- `langs/lang-spec.h` — interface header (issue 303)
255	- `issues/303-language-runtime-spec.md` — spec contract this implements
256	- `issues/304-task-dispatch-layer.md` — dispatch layer that calls
257	  `invoke`
258	- `issues/302-wire-value-slot-store.md` — large-value heap for
259	  variable-size returns
260	- `libs/dkjson` (vendored) — table-to-JSON serialization
261	- `maps/hello`, `maps/classify-demo` — Lua-only smoke test maps
262	
263	## Implementation log
264	
265	### Init + invoke + teardown — 2026-05-12
266	
267	`langs/lua/spec.c` now exports a real `lang_spec_t`:
268	
269	- `init(worker_idx)` creates a `lua_State` and opens the standard
270	  libraries via `luaL_openlibs`. Returns the state as the per-
271	  worker handle.
272	- `teardown(handle)` calls `lua_close`.
273	- `invoke(handle, file_path, fn_name, inputs, sizes, n, out_buf,
274	  out_cap, out_size)` loads the file with `luaL_loadfile`,
275	  executes the chunk (which is expected to return a module
276	  table), pulls the named function out of that table, pushes each
277	  input as a Lua string, calls via `lua_pcall`, and copies the
278	  return as a string into the caller's buffer. The state's stack
279	  is rewound to its baseline depth at every entry/exit so
280	  cross-call leaks don't accumulate. Errors at every step
281	  surface as nonzero return and a stderr message.
282	
283	Five end-to-end tests in `tests/306-lua-spec-test.c` exercise the
284	init/teardown round-trip, a `greet(name, salutation)` call with
285	both args, the same call with one arg (the Lua side defaults
286	salutation to "Hello"), and the two failure modes (missing
287	function, missing file). The test loads the spec through the
288	real spec registry via dlopen, so this is the actual artifact
289	the pool runner will consume.
290	
291	Deferred:
292	- **Rich return types.** The current invoke serializes the return
293	  with `lua_tolstring`, which handles strings/numbers/booleans/
294	  nil via tostring semantics. Tables, closures, and other
295	  structured values land with the fast-path work in issue 312.
296	
297	### Per-worker module cache — 2026-05-12
298	
299	`lua_init` now seeds an empty Lua table at
300	`LUA_REGISTRYINDEX[LUA_CACHE_KEY]`. `lua_invoke` looks up the
301	box's `file_path` in that table; on a hit it skips the
302	`luaL_loadfile + lua_pcall` round-trip and uses the cached
303	module table directly; on a miss it loads, runs the chunk,
304	verifies the module table shape, and stashes the result in the
305	cache before proceeding.
306	
307	The cache is per-worker (each worker has its own `lua_State`),
308	which means no locking. It's also per-file-path string, so two
309	boxes that share a source file share a module table on each
310	worker — calling `M.greet` from one box doesn't reload the
311	chunk when another box later calls `M.farewell` on the same
312	file.
313	
314	Cache invalidation is intentionally absent for now: once a file
315	is loaded, its module table sticks for the run. If the box
316	source changes mid-run, the worker won't notice. Fine for the
317	read-once-then-execute usage; revisit when there's a use case
318	for hot-reload.
319	
```

  "new_string":"## Input marshalling\n\nInputs arrive at `invoke` as `(bytes, size, is_native)` triples. The\n`is_native` bit per input is set by the graph loader's per-edge\nclassification (issue 312) — true when the producer feeding this port\nis a call box in the same language (Lua → Lua), false when bytes are\nJSON-encoded by an upstream of a different language (or by anyone\ndeliberately writing JSON).\n\n- `is_native == 1` → `lua_pushlstring(L, bytes, size)`. The user's\n  function sees the bytes as a Lua string and is free to call\n  `tonumber`, `tostring`, etc. on them — same convention as the\n  phase 2 driver.\n- `is_native == 0` → parse the bytes as JSON via the project parser\n  and push the resulting Lua value (table for arrays/objects,\n  number/string/boolean/nil for primitives). On parse failure the\n  bytes are pushed as a raw string so that producers still emitting\n  non-JSON keep working (transitional fallback inherited from\n  issue 312's slice 4).\n\nThe original \"declared input type\" idea — a per-port type tag that\nthe spec would honor — never shipped. Per-edge classification proved\nsufficient: the producer's language and the consumer's language are\nknown at load time, which is enough to decide native-or-JSON per\nedge. Adding a per-port type tag would have duplicated that decision\nin two places.",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/issues/306-lua-language-spec.md"
}

```
# 307 — C language spec implementation

## Status
open

## Current behavior
C boxes today run via `drivers/c.sh`, which compiles the source on
demand (mtime-based recompile to a binary), then spawns the binary as
a subprocess and reads stdout. Process spawn cost dominates; each call
is its own freshly-launched executable. The phase 2 driver compiles
to a standalone binary, not a `.so`.

## Concept
The C language spec implements `lang_spec_t` (issue 303) for C. It is
the second reference implementation, and the first one that exercises
the `compile` callback. After compile, invocation is in-process via
`dlopen` / `dlsym` — the user's compiled function is called directly
through a function pointer, with no subprocess and no IPC.

This is the "Option 1 — LuaJIT FFI" model from
`docs/004-ipc-and-threading.md`, applied to C: same-process call,
nanosecond-overhead invocation, native-typed access. The only thing
the C spec adds over the Lua spec is the compile step.

## Per-worker state

`init` returns a small struct holding the dlopen-handle cache:

```c
typedef struct {
    void  *so_handles[MAX_BOXES];   // per-file .so handle, indexed by file id
    void  *fn_ptrs   [MAX_BOXES];   // per-(file, fn) cached function pointer
    int    n_cached;
    // (a hash table could replace these arrays if MAX_BOXES grows large)
} c_handle_t;

void *c_init(int worker_idx);
void  c_teardown(void *handle);    // dlclose every cached handle
```

Each worker thread has its own cache. The same `.so` will end up
loaded once per worker, since `dlopen` with the same path on the same
process returns a refcounted handle (the underlying code segment is
shared across all callers — the per-worker `dlopen` is just a
refcount bump after the first).

## Compile callback

The C spec's `compile` callback receives the box's metadata in
addition to the source and output paths, so it knows the function's
signature and can generate the wrapper:

```c
int c_compile(const char *src_path, const char *out_path, const box_t *box);
```

(This is a small extension to `lang_spec_t` in issue 303 — `compile`
gains a `box_t` pointer. Lua's compile is NULL, so unaffected.)

The callback:
1. Reads `box->inputs`, `box->returns`, `box->headers`, `box->link_libs`,
   `box->cflags` from the box metadata.
2. Generates a wrapper `.c` file (see "Wrapper generation" below) in
   the map's `compiled/bin/` directory next to where the `.so` will
   land.
3. Forks and execs the system `cc` with:
   - `-shared -fPIC -O2 -Wall` (defaults)
   - The user's `cflags`, if any
   - The wrapper `.c` and the user's `src_path` (if `ref` is set)
   - `-o out_path`
   - `-l<lib>` for each entry in `box->link_libs`
4. Waits for the compiler, returns 0 on success, 1 on failure.

If the resulting `.so` is newer than the user's source and the
wrapper, compile is skipped (mtime check, same as phase 2).

The compiler is `cc` — the system default. `gcc` on most Linux,
`clang` on macOS. No SoraMech-specific compiler dependency.

### cflags in box JSON
The `cflags` field on the box JSON is a string of additional compiler
flags (`-O0 -g`, `-mavx2`, etc.). It is set by the user in the
inspector — a "Compile flags" text input shown when a C-language box
is selected. The editor saves it as part of the box's JSON; reads
preserve the field; saves write it back. Nothing overwrites it.

## Box file convention: any C function, no boilerplate

**The rule, stated as a rule:** a box author writes code as if
SoraMech does not exist. The function they write must be a
function their non-SoraMech compiler can compile, their
non-SoraMech editor can lint, their non-SoraMech test harness
can call. No SoraMech-prefixed macros. No SoraMech-shaped
return types. No `#include "soramech.h"`. Nothing in the
author's source betrays that the function will ever cross
SoraMech's wire.

SoraMech bends to the language. The language never bends to
SoraMech. This is non-negotiable across every spec; it's the
reason for the spec interface in the first place.
```
